Note the path-vocabulary fix (2026-08-11) knowingly added ~220 words to that file rather than lose the invariant; it did not create the violation.
- [x] **master is RED on Windows — 1 test remaining (was 7), from work merged 2026-08-11** `bug` `#native-runtime` `#ci` `#harness` (added 2026-08-11; **6 of 7 CLOSED 2026-08-12 by youcoded `16a9adb5`; ALL CLOSED 2026-08-15 — youcoded PR #317, merge `c4fdc19c`: Desktop CI green on Windows + macOS + Ubuntu for the first time since 2026-08-10**)
  **2026-08-15 close-out.** The `persistent_env` Windows failure was a REAL product bug, not a test defect: Git Bash's `mktemp` prints an MSYS path (`/tmp/tmp.XXXX`), Node on Windows resolves it as `C:\tmp\...`, the env dump was never read and the best-effort catch hid it — the probe now announces the path via `cygpath -w` (bash.ts). Also fixed in the same PR, none of them in this entry before: the "Windows without Git Bash" test really spawns `powershell.exe` and needed a 30 s timeout; `harness-eval-orchestrator.test.ts` had 78 Windows failures from `import('D:\...')` (`ERR_UNSUPPORTED_ESM_URL_SCHEME` — every computed-absolute-path `import()` in `test-engine/*.mjs` and the test's probes now goes through `pathToFileURL`), and once THAT was fixed vite's test runner mangled the `file:///D:/` form in-process, so `vitest.config.ts` now externalizes `test-engine/*.mjs` (native Node load — measured to also make `require()`/`import()` module identity agree, which the test's own comment had recorded as diverging); plus one test asserting a POSIX-shaped absolute path. Lesson stands: `verify.sh` is Linux-only, and it took three Windows CI round-trips to land — each one found the next layer.
  **Not the path-vocabulary bug** (that one is fixed, entry below). Two PRs merged onto an already-red master on 2026-08-11:
  - ~~`43a9c43a` → six `harness-review-runner.test.ts > wrap-up turn` failures~~ **CLOSED by `16a9adb5` ("fix(test): the red wrap-up tests are timing out, not failing")** — these were vitest 5,000ms-default timeouts on tests whose work doubled when `BATTERY_STEP_BUDGET` moved to 100; every failure sat at exactly 5,000ms. Not a native-runtime defect and not a mid-review-merge logic bug; the fix gives the nine tests explicit 30s timeouts, no production code touched.
  - `a2b0e35f` ("warn when shell state won't carry…") → `harness-tools-core.test.ts > Bash > env-state warning + opt-in persistence (persistent_env) > a persistent_env call carries an exported var to the NEXT call`, Windows-only. **Still open as of 2026-08-12** — no Windows guard, no skip, nothing since `a2b0e35f` touches it (`922fcd32` fixed a different pair of round-8 tests).
  Measured, not inferred: master at `a2b0e35f` had **11** Windows failures and **4** macOS; PR #291 cut that to **7** Windows and **0** macOS by fixing the four originals plus `workspace-root miss hint` (a NEW `a2b0e35f` test that depends on the cwd behavior #291 corrects). Run ids: master `31521789535`, PR `31522517435`.
  **Same lesson as the entry below, one day later:** `scripts/verify.sh` is Linux-only, so a green local run says nothing about the two platforms these broke on. Both PRs were merged over a red matrix.
- [x] **master was RED on Windows and macOS CI** — four Bash-cwd / Grep-path tests `bug` `#native-runtime` `#ci` `#harness` (added 2026-08-11; **FIXED 2026-08-11 — youcoded PR #291, merge `71c4014a`**. Spec + plan archived: `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-11-harness-cross-platform-path-vocabulary.md`)
  Broke at `eba51705` ("Merge harness tool honesty + review runner", 2026-08-10) and still failing at master tip. Ubuntu passes; the `build` aggregate passes. Failing tests, verified identical on master and on every branch cut from it — **four distinct cases across six platform/case combinations**, not six tests:
  - **Windows:** `Bash reports the tracked cwd after a cd, so the model never has to guess` · `ANSI stripping does not disturb the cwd sentinel` · `Grep returns workspace-relative paths for targets inside the workspace`
  - **macOS:** `Bash always states the cwd and exit code` · `Bash reports the tracked cwd after a cd…` · `ANSI stripping does not disturb the cwd sentinel`
  The first three are in `tests/harness-tools-core.test.ts` (`:468`, `:491`, `:521`); the Grep case is in `tests/harness-tool-bounds.test.ts:53`.
  **CORRECTED 2026-08-11 — the original `__YC_CWD__` sentinel hypothesis below was WRONG.** The sentinel works correctly on all three platforms. These are **two independent defects**, both real product bugs, neither a test defect: (1) `bash.ts:499` compares the shell's reported cwd to `startCwd` with `path.resolve`, which neither follows symlinks nor expands Windows 8.3 short names — so two spellings of ONE directory read as a change and the canonical form lands in `setShellCwd` and the `[cwd: …]` metadata line (macOS `/var`→`/private/var`; Windows `RUNNER~1`→`runneradmin`). Reproduced on **Linux** with a symlinked root, so this is not a CI-only artifact — it hits any symlinked workspace root on any platform. (2) `glob.ts:232` normalizes separators to `/`; `grep.ts` prints ripgrep's stdout verbatim and rg uses the platform separator, so Windows gets `src\a.ts` from Grep and `src/a.ts` from Glob — the sibling Glob test passing on Windows is the proof Grep is the outlier. Fix, decisions, rejected alternatives, and the traps (rebasing must not outrun the scope guard; `--path-separator=/` alone silently breaks the currently-agreeing external-directory case; `guards.ts:25 canonicalize()` must NOT be reused for output because it lowercases on Windows) are all in the spec.
  **Why this matters beyond the red X:** the desktop CI is the ONLY thing exercising these two platforms — `scripts/verify.sh` runs on the developer's machine, which is Linux, so a local green says nothing about them. Two PRs (#289, #290) were merged on 2026-08-11 with these failures present, after confirming the same failures on master first; they are inherited, not caused. But the longer master stays red the less any future red means, which is the same "trains sessions to ignore the unignorable check" failure already recorded for the `web-fetch.ts` ast-grep entry.
- [x] **Sync health warnings never clear themselves — "No internet" sat under "All synced · 1m ago"** `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-08-11, reported by Destin from his live app, **SHIPPED 2026-08-11 — youcoded PR #292, merge `883bf3ad`**)
  `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` ran **exactly once per app run**, at the end of `start()`. It owns three codes — `OFFLINE`, `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `PERSONAL_STALE` — and all three describe conditions that change *while the app is open*, but nothing re-evaluated them. A launch that lost the race with the WiFi coming up (or any resolver hiccup — the probe is a single `dns.lookup('github.com')` with a 5s timeout) wrote `OFFLINE` into `.sync-warnings.json`, and it stayed there for the rest of the session. Destin's screenshot is the two states side by side: a red "No internet — can't reach the network" card directly under a green "All synced · GitHub · itsdestin · instant sync on · 1m ago" header, four devices listed, self "Synced just now".
  This is the residue the **PR #254 entry below predicted in its own last sentence** — *"Landing #248 alone does NOT close this: any transient warning (a real OFFLINE at launch) still pins the row red until restart."* #254 fixed the renderer half (the Settings row read a frozen mount-time snapshot instead of the live file); after it, every surface reads the file faithfully — and the **file** was the stale thing. Worth noting as a pattern: three separate entries here (#248, #254, this one) are the same bogus-warning symptom at three different layers, each fix correct and each insufficient alone.
  Fix: re-run the check every 60s, cleared in `stop()`; the periodic run passes `probeBackends: false` so `autoDetectBackend`'s rclone shell-out (35s, measured on the Z13) stays a launch-only cost; `OFFLINE` now needs two consecutive failed probes so a launch race can't produce it at all; a dismissed code stays dismissed for the app run (else the re-check resurrects it a minute later); the file is rewritten only when the set actually changes, with `createdEpoch` excluded from that comparison — which also upgrades the field's meaning to "when this condition was first seen". Guard: `tests/sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts`, 9 cases, all red on the parent commit; the load-bearing one is "start() keeps re-checking, and stop() ends it", since the sweep logic itself was never the missing piece.
- [x] **A permission prompt can describe a DIFFERENT tool than the one it approves** — *raises the priority of M5 item 3 (no way to revoke an "Always allow"); see program §6* `bug` `#native-runtime` `#chat-reducer` `#permissions` (added 2026-08-09, Destin during the M1–M3 dogfood; **merged 2026-08-10, PR #287**)
  Reported sequence, native session: `Bash 1 → "Allow Bash 1?" → Bash 1 active → Read 1 → "Allow Bash 1?" → Read 1 active`. The card names and describes the earlier tool; approving it runs the new one. **This is a consent bug, not a display bug** — the user is shown one action and authorizes another, on the app's highest-stakes control. Treat it as higher severity than its "sometimes" framing suggests.
  **Mechanism, confirmed present on master (not reproduced, but the code path is unambiguous).** `PERMISSION_REQUEST` binds an incoming ask to an existing card through a three-tier match (`chat-reducer.ts:1135-1162`): (1) same name AND identical input, (2) first running tool with the same name, (3) **`anyRunningId` — the first running tool of ANY name.** Tier 3 is the bug: it hands the ask to an unrelated card, and the code comment at `:1177-1179` records that swapping the input in was *deliberately declined* there ("would render tool Y's name over tool X's input, a worse lie"). So tier 3 knowingly leaves the earlier tool's name AND input on a card whose buttons now authorize a different tool. Native asks reach this same reducer path — they ride the CC `hook:event` channel by design (`ipc-handlers.ts:2372`) into `hook-dispatcher.ts:25` — so this is NOT native-specific, though native is where it was seen.
  **Two conditions must coincide, which is why it is intermittent:** a tool of a different name is still `status: 'running'`, AND the new tool's own card does not exist yet, so tiers 1–2 miss. **The first condition is very likely the stuck-"running" bug logged directly below** — an orphaned card poisons this matcher for the rest of the session. Fixing that one may reduce this one's frequency without touching the real hole; do not let that mask it.
  **Answering "did we already fix this?": partly, and not this part.** `a4232a18` (merged 2026-08-09, `fix/askuserquestion-stale-card`) fixed the adjacent tier-2 case — a name-matched card showing an earlier call's input, which is how the second AskUserQuestion of a session re-displayed the first one's options. Tier 3 was consciously excluded from that fix. Destin's installed build predates it regardless.
  **FIXED — merged 2026-08-10 on PR #287 (youcoded `fad49656`).** The gating question is answered: `git show d6c90c1a^` shows tier 3 WAS the original naive implementation (its own comment: "the arbitrary first-running-tool fallback"); tiers 1–2 were added in front of it and it was never removed. Vestigial, not load-bearing — deleted. An unmatched ask now falls through to the pre-existing synthetic card, which describes the ask's own payload. Guard: `chat-reducer.test.ts` → "PERMISSION_REQUEST tool identity".
  **Original fix direction, kept for the record:** an ask should never bind to a card whose `toolName` differs from the ask's. Dropping tier 3 lets it fall through to the synthetic-card path that already exists at `:1203`, which mints a card from the ask's own payload and is reclaimed by `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_USE`'s merge when the real event lands — honest by construction. **Before removing it, establish why tier 3 was added** (tier 2 already degrades on input mismatch, so tier 3 only earns its place if some producer reports a name the transcript does not — MCP's `mcp__server__tool` namespacing is the obvious suspect). If such a case exists, reconcile the names rather than keeping a blind fallback.
- [x] **`bash scripts/ast-grep/check.sh` is RED on `master`** `bug` `#tooling` (added 2026-08-07, **FIXED 2026-08-06/09 — youcoded `feb76640`, merged via `8c62ca51`. check.sh re-verified green 2026-08-12 (5/5 fixture rules fire, zero real-source violations).**)
  **The entry's premise was wrong: the rule was RIGHT.** `tool-bounds-not-hand-rolled` was not over-broadened and never matched a "fragment-resolution note" — it was correctly firing on two genuine hand-rolled `[body truncated at 5MB]` strings in `web-fetch.ts:160,169`. The fix (`feb76640`, "WebFetch overstated what it showed") removed the hand-rolled strings from the tool so the cap is declared via bounds; no rule carve-out was needed. The only remaining regex-shaped text in the file lives inside `//` WHY comments, which the AST rule doesn't match.
- [x] **`ToolCard` interpolates `unknown`-typed tool inputs straight into chat-visible strings** `bug` `#renderer` (added 2026-08-06, from the 2026-08-06 lint sweep; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #295, merge `3dac8828`.** Turned out much bigger than the three named sites: the review found the same class INCLUDED live crashes — `basename(input.path as string)` in Grep/Glob/Read/Write/Edit branches, `.replace`/`.trimStart`/`.includes` on lie-cast strings — any of which took down the whole Chat ErrorBoundary pane. Every interpolated field in `friendlyToolDisplay` now goes through an `asString()` helper or a typeof guard; 21 tests, all red-first. Twin shape in `tool-views/ToolBody.tsx` + `AskUserQuestionCard` deep fields ticketed separately below.)
  `ToolCard.tsx:99/103/120` render `input.glob`, `input.type` and `input.subagent_type` into the tool card label/detail with no string cast, while the sibling lines in the same switch DO cast (`input.path as string`, `input.pattern as string`). These are `unknown` off the tool-input JSON, so a non-string value renders as `[object Object]` in the timeline. Low severity — the models emit strings in practice — but it is a three-line fix and the inconsistency with the adjacent lines is the tell. Caught by `@typescript-eslint/no-base-to-string`, which is in the deferred list above (13 hits total).
- [x] **Resume Browser takes ~1s to open and ~280ms per search keystroke — the list is not windowed** `bug` `#renderer` `#performance` (added + shipped 2026-07-31, `f8ca631b`, merged in youcoded#279)
  Destin: "it definitely does feel slow… i've been wanting to optimize it for performance for a while." Measured, not estimated, at his real scale of ~1,642 rows: **948ms** from click to committed list (**37,920 DOM nodes**), **278ms** per search keystroke; against 225ms/546 nodes/48ms with 5 rows. `ResumeBrowser.tsx:1193` maps every row on every render with no virtualization, ~23 DOM nodes per card, and `renderSessionRow` is an unmemoized closure so all 1,642 subtrees reconcile on any state change.
  **File IO is NOT the cause and is already ruled out** — the whole of `listPastSessions()` measures **0.06s** against the real `~/.claude` tree (walk+stat 0.01, tail reads 0.01, head reads 0.03, store list 0.01). An earlier suggestion to skip the transcript scrape for store-covered rows would save ~10ms of a ~1000ms problem; don't.
  **Fix (2026-07-31): chunked reveal, not virtualization.** The list renders 50 items and an IntersectionObserver sentinel tops it up by 50 as the user scrolls. Matched before/after at 1,642 rows, median of 5 runs: open **804ms → 96ms** (script 425→72, style 194→13, layout 99→8), DOM nodes **37,920 → 1,585**, keystrokes **220/319/183ms → 64/83/100ms**. Open time is now **flat in conversation count** (90ms at 5 rows, 96ms at 1,642, 85ms at 4,000).
  Two pre-measurement hypotheses were **disproved** and should not be re-derived: `React.memo` on the row could never have fixed the OPEN (a first render has nothing to reuse — all the script and style cost still happens), and the filter pipeline never needed debouncing (keystroke cost shows no trend with session count). Remaining trade-off, accepted deliberately: the scrollbar is proportional to what is revealed, and scrolling through many hundreds of rows re-accumulates DOM. True windowing is the upgrade if deep scrolling ever becomes a real usage pattern.
  Full diagnosis, the two counting traps (subagent transcripts inflate the transcript count 2.2×; all 548 browsable transcripts DO have store records), and the reproducible harness: `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-07-31-resume-browser-load-time-handoff.md`.
- [x] `McpManager` keys holders by session id, so a resumed session's release closes the connection its own re-acquire just took `bug` `#native-runtime` `#mcp` (added 2026-07-31, found in the native-MCP phase 1 final review)
  A resumed session reuses its session id. `NativeSessionHost.destroy()` releases the outgoing generation while `create()`/`resume()` acquires for the incoming one, under the same id — and `McpManager`'s holder set is keyed by that id, so it cannot tell the two generations apart. The shipped mitigation defers the close until the owning `acquire()` settles, then runs it: the incoming session is handed a live connection that closes one microtask later, and its tool calls return `"<server> is not connected."` for the rest of the session.
  That is a deliberate trade, not an oversight — the alternative (skip the close when an acquire is in flight) leaks the spawned subprocess, which three regression tests in `mcp-manager.test.ts` pin. This same bug was found and relocated three times during phase 1 before landing here; the reason it keeps moving is that the id is the wrong key.
  **Fix:** `acquire()` returns an opaque handle and `release()` takes it, so two generations of one session id are distinguishable. Comment recording the exact limitation is at `mcp-manager.ts`'s `release()`.
- [x] Ten atomic-write call sites still use a FIXED `<file>.tmp`, which is not atomic against a concurrent writer `bug` `#desktop` `#reliability` (added 2026-08-05, found while merging native-MCP phase 1; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #296, merge `0a325b99`, plus workspace guard commit `e9ca14b`.** All eleven sites (the ten plus `github-client.ts` and `project-manager.ts` which the original count missed) now use pid-suffixed temp names; the review caught that pid names turn crash orphans PERMANENT, so cas-write and the two user-tree writers gained `sweepStaleTmp` (transcript-mirror's pattern) + error-path unlinks; `update-installer.ts`'s `.partial` deliberately kept fixed (load-bearing for crash recovery). The wished-for ast-grep rule EXISTS now (`atomic-tmp-name-per-process`, fires 13/13 on the old shapes, silent on fixed source). Two parked follow-ups: the `*.tmp`-in-DEFAULT_IGNORES product decision (own entry above) and optionally tightening the sweep match to `^base(\.\d+(\.\d+)?)?\.tmp$`.)
  Write-temp-then-rename protects against a crash mid-write. It does NOT protect against a second writer: both processes write the same temp path, the first rename consumes it, and the second throws `ENOENT` renaming a file that no longer exists. This is reachable in normal use — `~/.claude/` is SHARED between the installed app and a dev instance (`run-dev.sh` isolates userData and ports, not the home dir), so two YouCoded processes writing the same config file is the ordinary case, not a corner one.
  `skill-config-store.ts` was fixed on the native-MCP branch because it blocked that merge (its parallel-worker failure is the same two-writer shape). The remaining sites, verified by `rg` over `src/main` (re-verified 2026-08-12): `claude-code-registry.ts:119`, `marketplace-config-store.ts:41`, `marketplace-auth-store.ts:84`, `announcement-service.ts:33`, `artifacts/cas-write.ts:75`, `changelog-service.ts:50`, `sync-spaces/space-manager.ts:86`, `integration-installer.ts:93`, `ipc-handlers.ts:3717` (was :3576) — **plus two sites the original count missed: `github-client.ts:217` and `artifacts/project-manager.ts:67-68`**, so eleven total, not ten.
  The fix is mechanical and the convention already exists in this codebase — `mcp-reconciler.ts`, `hook-reconciler.ts`, `saved-folders.ts` and `transcript-mirror.ts` all use `${path}.${process.pid}.tmp`. Worth an ast-grep rule so the fixed-`.tmp` shape can't come back.
- [x] `window-all-closed` is the app's ONLY quit hook — Cmd+Q, dock quit and SIGTERM skip every teardown `bug` `#desktop` `#lifecycle` (added 2026-07-30, found in the native-MCP phase 1 Task 4 review)
  `main.ts` registers exactly one quit-related listener: `app.on('window-all-closed')` (`main.ts:1915-1942`), which calls `ipc-handlers.ts`'s `cleanup()`. There is **no** `before-quit`, `will-quit`, `quit` or `SIGTERM` handler anywhere in the file — verified by a repo-wide search during the Task 4 review, not inferred. So any quit route that does not go through window-all-closed first runs none of the teardown: macOS Cmd+Q and dock-quit call `app.quit()` directly (firing `before-quit`/`will-quit`/`quit`), and an OS-shutdown/logout SIGTERM bypasses Electron's quit events entirely on every platform.
  **What leaks:** `sessionManager.destroyAll()`, `hookRelay.stop()`, and — as of the native-MCP branch — `mcpManager.destroyAll()`, whose whole purpose is keeping a stdio MCP server's spawned subprocess from outliving the app. An MCP subprocess surviving a Cmd+Q is a real user-facing bug, not a theoretical one, and it is the exact failure the MCP teardown step was written to prevent.
  Pre-existing and app-wide, so deliberately NOT patched inside the MCP branch — the MCP teardown correctly hooks in beside the others. Fix is a `before-quit`/`will-quit` handler (idempotent with the existing one, since both can fire) plus a SIGTERM handler; `native-session-host.ts`'s `destroyAll()` carries a comment recording the exact scope limit so a future reader does not mistake the hook for full coverage.
- [x] `HookRelay.closeSocket()` has zero call sites `bug` `#permissions` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-31, found by the permission-ask-timeout final review) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  Dead code, and knip does not flag it because it is an exported class method. It is symmetric with Android's `EventBridge.closeSocket()`, which also has no callers — so either both are a deliberate API surface for a cross-path cleanup that was never wired, or both should go. The timeout branch kept it correct (it clears its hold timer) rather than deleting it mid-feature.
- [x] Desktop app burns CPU continuously at idle — measure, attribute, fix `bug` `#perf` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-30, **fixed 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #275; PR #274 closed, premise disproven**)
  Root cause was **not** the lease/heartbeat lead (renderer JS 99% idle, `pty:output` 0 bytes). It is a platform property: **on a high-refresh display, ANY smoothly-animating element makes Chromium present a frame per refresh at ~1.5–1.9ms of CPU each — ~29% of one core at 180Hz — regardless of element size or property.** Not app-specific: the identical pulsing div measured 29.8% in YouCoded, **33.7% in a bare Electron window with zero app code, and 26.7% in plain Google Chrome**. Cost is per-FRAME, not per-element (8 divs ≈ 1 div; an 8px layer-promoted transform animation still cost 31.9%).
  **Correction trail:** PR #274 shipped `will-change` layer promotion claiming 12× (43% → 3.6%); re-measurement disproved it (29.8% → 27.8%) — the original "promoted" reading was taken against a freshly-restored window that was not yet presenting (same artifact as the acknowledged bogus 0 fps reading). An early steps() test was likewise contaminated by probe-window occlusion. Lesson: **window presentation state is part of any perf experiment — re-run the baseline in the same window state before trusting an A/B.**
  The levers that actually work, both shipped in **PR #275**: (1) **fewer frames** — `steps()` timing on decorative animations (smooth 28.75% → `steps(8)` 9.33%): `.animate-pulse`/breathe/challenge-pulse at steps(8) (visually indistinguishable), `.animate-spin` at steps(12) (deliberate 12-tick spinner look). This is the per-animation form of Destin's "cap it to 30fps" suggestion — which was directionally right; there is no app-global knob (Electron `setFrameRate` is offscreen-only, Chromium switches only remove caps). (2) **zero frames** — Reduced Effects now actually stops CSS animations (`data-reduced-effects` previously had ZERO css rules gating on it; 48.78% → 2.44%), and `.flowing-word` is finite (plays twice, settles — its `background-position` paint is main-thread, immune to both promotion and steps()). Original 35.6% repro scenario: now **11.0%** with the animation running, ~2.5% with none.
  Also corrected from the handoff: the 104–133% live-app readings were **not idle** (the measuring session ran inside the app alongside 6 other streaming CC sessions ≈ 99% of a core); hidden windows were always fine (Chromium throttles them, 3.9% minimized) — the problem is exclusively visible-and-idle. 180Hz machines pay 3× a 60Hz laptop.
  Guards: `desktop/tests/animation-frame-budget.test.ts` (pins every quantization + bans new inline smooth-infinite animations in components/), `desktop/tests/reduced-effects-animations.test.ts`, `scripts/measure-idle-cpu.mjs` (flags SMOOTH animations vs `frame-budgeted: steps(N)`; refuses to sample the packaged app). Write-up incl. full correction history: docs/archive/investigations/2026-07-30-idle-cpu-burn.md.
  Follow-ups: ~~`model-load-sweep` animates `left`~~ and ~~mascot/buddy SVG keyframes~~ **both closed 2026-08-07** (branch `perf/transcript-hover-frames`) — the sweep now uses `translateX` (no layout pass), and the mascot's five rig loops plus its 33ms limb interval now pause on `visibilitychange` rather than being quantized, since `steps()` on character motion reads as juddering. `buddy-breathe` is deliberately NOT fixed: the buddy window is `alwaysOnTop`, so `visibilitychange` never fires, and pausing breathing makes the character look dead — recorded as an accepted cost in `ANIMATION_EXCEPTIONS` (`animation-frame-budget.test.ts`); ~~three full-rate rAF loops~~ (all fixed in PR #275 — BrailleSpinner/ThemeEffects on intervals, MascotRig dual-rate with rAF only while dragging; rig themes were presenting 180 sway frames/sec since idleSway never lets springs park); a session that never initializes still shows a permanent "Initializing session…" overlay (UX bug, no longer a CPU one); and whether this machine's per-frame cost is inflated by the missing `DC_DISABLE_IPS` amdgpu flag (measure after the kernel-param fix, or at 60Hz).
- [x] `.flowing-word` keyword gradient burns ~40% of a core whenever a keyword is on screen `bug` `#perf` `#desktop` (added 2026-07-30, **fixed 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #275**)
  `.flowing-word` (used by `FlowingKeywords.tsx` + `UserMessage.tsx`) animated `background-position` on an infinite loop — main-thread painted, so **no rendering trick helps**: plain 43.8%, `will-change: background-position` 43.0%, forced layer via `will-change: transform` 65.9% (vs 66.1% unpromoted), `steps(24)` still ~40%. Fixed by making it **finite**: plays twice on appearance (~6s of flow, preserving the draw-the-eye purpose), then settles to the static start gradient — screenshot-verified legible. Cost after settling: zero. Reduced Effects stops it entirely. Guarded by `animation-frame-budget.test.ts` (`infinite` on this rule fails the suite). The fancier alternative (transform-driven gradient overlay behind `background-clip: text`) is no longer needed for perf; only worth revisiting if Destin wants the endless-flow look back.
- [x] Sync transport converts git failures into success — a crash-corrupted repo syncs "green" forever `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, **MERGED 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #276, merge 5a787879**; spec + plan archived to `docs/archive/`)
  Found live on the Z13: three hard freezes (Jul 27–29, the EC-wedge machine) left 16 zero-byte loose objects in the Personal space's `sync.git`; `refs/heads/main` pointed at one of them, so every git op failed `fatal: bad object HEAD` — and **sync was dead for three days while the panel showed green and `lastSync` advanced every cycle** (3,381 files staged, never pushed). Two defects: (1) `git-transport.ts` `git()` never throws and `push()`/`pull()` map failures to `{pushed:false}`/`{updated:false}` — indistinguishable from "nothing to do" — so the engine emits phantom `synced` events that stamp `recordSyncSuccess` (verified by reproducing the transport's exact command sequence against the corrupt repo: commit exits 128 → silent benign return). Only auth failures deliberately surface; every other mode falls into the trap `classifyGitAuthFailure`'s own WHY comment warns about. (2) Zero-byte objects are *poison*: git checks existence by filename, so `add -A` keeps "succeeding" and never rewrites them from the intact worktree — corruption never self-heals. Related third defect, same session (screenshot): the devices-list **self** row reads legacy `.sync-marker` (absent on GitHub-era installs) → "last seen 22 hours ago" on a running machine, while peer rows ride the live SyncHub map; `syncInProgress` stats the legacy `.sync-lock` identically. Approved design (all four parts): benign-allowlist inversion (`classifyGitFailure` — non-zero exit ⇒ error unless allowlisted; corrupt patterns throw coded `repo-corrupt`), two-tier auto-repair (surgical zero-byte purge + reset to `origin/main`, escalate to backup-aside + refetch; once per space per launch; worktree never touched), notice/danger surfacing via existing panel plumbing, and the self-row rewire onto `lastSyncFor` evidence. Sequencing: land PR #248 (same legacy-state family, open since 07-24) first, then honesty before the row rewire — the row is only as truthful as the events feeding it. Z13's own repo to be repaired by hand in the same session (local history keepable — `ca2f6a0c` verified intact; pending app quit).
- [x] Renderer maps neither `repo-corrupt` nor `repo-repair-failed`, and its fallback copy is wrong for one of them `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #299, merge `a8b0a290`**: both codes now have explicit mappings with restart-based copy whose every claim the review traced to code — repair writes only under `.youcoded/`, `healedSpaces` is in-memory so restart genuinely retries, and the offline-corrupt corner (separate entry below) surfaces different copy via a different path so nothing overpromises.)
  `summarizeSpaceSyncError` (`sync-space-error-summary.ts`) special-cases only `'github-auth'`, so both error codes PR #276 introduced fall to the generic tail: *"Sync hit an unexpected problem. It will keep retrying automatically."* For `repo-corrupt` that is merely vague. For `repo-repair-failed` it is **reassuring and wrong within the launch** — the sync does keep retrying, but the heal is guarded to one attempt per space per launch and will not run again. Accurate copy is closer to "Sync couldn't repair itself — restart the app to try again." Renderer work was explicitly out of #276's scope; this is the highest-priority of its follow-ups. Both codes are exported constants (`REPO_CORRUPT_ERROR_CODE`, `REPO_REPAIR_FAILED_ERROR_CODE`) — match on those, never on prose.
- [x] `repair()` leaves no trace of which tier ran `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-30, from the PR #276 whole-branch review; **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #299, merge `a8b0a290`**: `repair()` returns + logs a `RepairOutcome` {tier, zeroByteObjectsDeleted, tier1Failure, backupPath} via the subsystem's injected logFn; git/fs sequence verified byte-identical. Known residual asymmetry, deliberate: a Tier-2 *failure* exits before the tier-2 log line — the engine's "Sync self-repair failed" error event still carries the cause.)
  On crash-prone hardware "did the heal fire, and which one" is the first question a debug session asks, and nothing on disk answers it: `deleteZeroByteObjects`'s return count is discarded, Tier 1 and Tier 2 emit the **identical** notice, and the only durable artifact is the `.broken-*` directory, which `pruneBrokenBackups` reduces to one. If a user later reports "my sync history changed", there is no record. `service.ts` already has a `log` in scope at engine construction — one line per tier closes it. Smaller items in the same file while there: `tests/sync-spaces-repair.test.ts`'s `cleanup()` is not in a `finally`/`afterEach`, so a failing assertion leaks a temp git repo; `brokenBackupName` has one-second resolution, so two repairs of the same space within one second `ENOTEMPTY` the rename (non-destructive — surfaces as `repo-repair-failed`); and `getSelfLastSyncEpochMs` does a `readdir` plus one uncached `readFileSync` + `JSON.parse` **per space** on every 10s status tick (`SpaceManager.read()` is uncached), which wants a single read + max.
- [x] Sound presets cannot be auditioned without assigning them `bug` `#ui` (added 2026-07-26, **SHIPPED 2026-07-26** on `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1`, unmerged)
  `PresetSelector` (`SettingsPanel.tsx:340-351`) fires `onSelect(p.id); playPreview(p.id)` in the same handler, so **the only way to hear a stock sound is to make it your notification sound.** Shopping through the library means clobbering the setting 15 times and then remembering which one you wanted. Compounding it: all **15** presets (`utils/sounds.ts:133-149`) render as `px-2 py-1 rounded text-[10px]` chips in a `flex-wrap`, **twice** — once per category — so the list is both unreadable and duplicated. Fix is in the menu-internals spec §4: one library listed once, a K3 segmented control picking *which slot you are filling* (Needs attention / Response ready), a Radio to assign, and a separate `▶` icon button to audition. Optional polish in the same edit: `SoundPreset` gains a `desc` field and the tone signatures that already exist as trailing comments on `STOCK_PRESETS` ("C5 → E5", "A4 double tap") lift into it — strings unchanged; without it the 15 rows are hard to tell apart. Small and self-contained; does not need the rest of the design system to land first.
  **Outcome (2026-07-26):** shipped, but NOT as specced. Destin reviewed the first fix — Radio to assign, separate `▶` to audition — and reversed it: with one shared list behind a category toggle, assigning IS how you listen, so a second control was just another thing to aim at. Selecting a sound now plays it, and the whole tile is the hit target. `desc` shipped. The guard test inverted accordingly (`tests/sound-preview.test.ts` asserts selecting DOES preview). Also fixed in the same pass: the popup could not scroll at all, which turned out to be a `SettingsPopup` defect affecting Session Defaults too — the trigger for the whole D1 shell rework.
- [x] Settings "Backup & Sync" row freezes on launch-time warnings for the whole app run `bug` `#sync` `#ui` (added 2026-07-26, reported by Destin from his live app, **SHIPPED 2026-07-26 — youcoded PR #254, merge `6645db83`**)
  Symptom: the Settings row reads red **"Sync Failing"** with a `2` badge while the Backup & Sync popup two clicks away reads green **"All synced"** and shows no Warnings section — and `~/.claude/.sync-warnings.json` does not exist on disk. Root cause is a refresh gap, not a disagreement between the two derivations (both correctly route through `deriveSyncBoxState`/`deriveSettingsRowState`). `SyncSection` (`SyncPanel.tsx:272`) is mounted from **app launch**, not from settings-open — `DesktopSettings` renders unconditionally inside the always-mounted, translate-hidden drawer (`SettingsPanel.tsx:242,270`) — and it calls `getStatus()` exactly once, 350 ms in. Nothing ever refetches `status.warnings` after that: its `status:data` handler deliberately patches only `lastSyncEpoch`/`syncInProgress`/`backupMeta`/`lastSyncByDevice` (`SyncPanel.tsx:296-315`), and the only other caller — the popup's `refreshStatus` → `onRefresh` — auto-fires solely when the LEGACY `toolkit-state/.sync-marker` epoch advances, a file that does not exist on a spaces-only install, so `advanced` is false forever. Meanwhile `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` runs ~35 s after launch (the `rclone lsd` probe is slow — Z13 log: start `12:40:06`, `Auto-detected sync backend: drive` at `12:40:41`) and rewrites the warnings file, sweeping every stale health code. So the row reads the file as the PREVIOUS session left it and then keeps showing that snapshot for the rest of the app's uptime, while every fresh-reading surface (StatusBar pill off the 10 s `status:data` push, the popup off its own `getStatus()`) correctly shows nothing. The 07-25 22:06 launch logged no auto-detect line → `autoDetectBackend()` returned null → the phantom pair is almost certainly `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED` + `OFFLINE`, both danger, both health codes, both swept at 12:40:41 — i.e. the ROADMAP entry below (PR #248) is the SOURCE of the bogus warnings and this entry is why they outlive their own fix by a full app session. Fix: have `SyncSection` take warnings from the authoritative 10 s push (`statusData.syncWarnings`, already read straight off `.sync-warnings.json` by `buildStatusData`, `ipc-handlers.ts:1884`) instead of its frozen mount-time snapshot — App.tsx's gear danger-dot already does exactly this (`App.tsx:1823-1845`). Landing #248 alone does NOT close this: any transient warning (a real OFFLINE at launch) still pins the row red until restart.
- [x] "No sync configured" fires on a machine whose GitHub sync is healthy `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-24, **MERGED 2026-07-30 — youcoded PR #248, merge 0a5b15b9**)
  Found by Destin on a fresh macOS install running `1.3.0-beta.9`: red "Sync Failing" StatusBar chip + non-dismissible "No sync configured — your backups aren't set up", with GitHub sync set up, connected and green two clicks away. Root cause: `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` only reads the LEGACY additional-backup backends (`storage_backends` in `toolkit-state/config.json` — Drive/iCloud/rclone). Sync spaces keeps its own state at `toolkit-state/sync-spaces.json` and is invisible to that read, so zero legacy backends = "no sync" regardless of the primary system. **Clean installs are exactly the affected population, which is why this survived to beta.9:** `autoDetectBackend()` masks it on old machines — its `rclone lsd gdrive:Claude/Backup/` probe still exits 0 on a box that used the legacy backups years ago, silently skipping the warning without enabling any backend (verified on the Z13: `storage_backends` is `[]`, rclone exits 0, no warnings file on disk). New machines have no rclone and no `~/Library/…/Claude/Backup`, so they take the warning. Fix gates the warning on the primary system, rewords `PERSONAL_NOT_CONFIGURED` off "connect a cloud provider", and renames `PERSONAL_STALE` to name the Drive/iCloud markers it actually tracks. Health codes are re-cleared every launch, so affected installs self-heal on the next start. Flip to `[x]` on merge.
- [x] Artifact tracker writes RELATIVE paths into `absolutePath` (documented canonical-absolute) `bug` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-23, **MERGED 2026-08-13 — youcoded master 3213c0af**)
  **Shipped.** Plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-12-artifact-relative-path-misclassification.md`. Root cause confirmed as predicted below — the NATIVE harness `Write`/`Edit`/`Read` accept a relative `file_path` and resolve it with `path.resolve(ctx.cwd, p)` themselves, but the transcript event carries the RAW arg, and `resolveTrackedPath` had no branch for it. Four parts: classifier fix; an `isAbsoluteRecorded` guard at every site that builds a path from a record (**five, not the four the plan first counted** — `renameArtifact` was an unguarded `fs.rename`); a pure one-time repair that re-runs stored paths through the fixed classifier and merges collisions; and wiring gated on `reclassified === 0` with a `.pre-migration.bak` written first. Dry-run against a copy of the real 2,855-record sidecar: 48 reclassified, 13 merged, six invariants held, idempotent second pass. Full desktop suite 5,070 green; Android 171/171. **Known gap shipping with it: Android never gets the repair** — see the entry below.
  Found auditing the real `.youcoded/artifacts.json`: ≥7 external records (re-counted 2026-08-12: now **15**, still accumulating — new ones from the Aug work) have a non-absolute `absolutePath` — `docs/MAP.md`, `flappy-bird/styles.css`, `youcoded/desktop/src/renderer/state/chat-reducer.ts`, etc. The `ArtifactRecord.absolutePath` field is documented "canonical, set when kind='external'" (`shared/artifacts/types.ts`), and `visible-artifacts.ts`/`import-file` canonicalize it as absolute. A relative value only "resolves" by luck against the current cwd — `fs.access('docs/MAP.md')` passes when the app's cwd happens to be the workspace root, and fails everywhere else, so these records flip between present and orphaned by cwd. Something in the tracker (App.tsx artifact-tracker path, likely a Write/Edit tool-use whose path arrives project-relative) is storing the raw path instead of resolving it against the project root first. Latent regardless of the Files-tab work; surfaced by it. (found 2026-07-23)
- [x] Nothing detects an artifact repair that silently over-reclassifies `chore` `#artifacts` (added 2026-08-13) **DONE 2026-09-01** — youcoded PR #376 — `migrateRelativeExternals` returns a `reclassifiedFrom` trail; the sidecar migration logs count, list and backup path.
  On first launch after the relative-path fix ships, ~48 records on `youcoded-dev` become newly VISIBLE in Project View (externals are pin-gated by `visible-artifacts.ts:62`, internals are not). That is the intended fix, but if the classifier ever reclassified a shape the dry-run did not contain, the symptom is phantom in-project files, not an error — nothing would surface it but Destin noticing unfamiliar entries. `.youcoded/artifacts.json.pre-migration.bak` is the recovery path. Also unlogged: a repair that fails once is memoized for the whole process (it does now `console.error`, but nothing surfaces that to the user).
- [x] Kotlin `detectOrphan` is dead code with test-only callers `chore` `#android` (added 2026-08-13) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**

- [x] Remote WS set-tag/set-note don't broadcast `SESSION_META_CHANGED` on success `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-23) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  Found in M2 final review (youcoded PR #212): the desktop IPC surface's meta-write path broadcasts `SESSION_META_CHANGED` after a successful persist (the M2 broadcast-after-persist fix), but the remote WS `set-tag`/`set-note` handlers persist without the matching broadcast — a second remote client (or the same session viewed from another device) goes stale until its next full refresh. Pre-existing gap, not introduced by M2; M2 only fixed the desktop-IPC honesty half. **Partial adjacent work landed since (re-checked 2026-08-12): both handlers now call `emitConversationMetaChanged()` (the chatsearch signal) — but still no `remoteServer.broadcast({type: SESSION_META_CHANGED})`, which exists only on the IPC path (`ipc-handlers.ts:3067, 3089`).**
- [x] `SESSION_CREATE` native-resume split-refusal branch has no direct test `bug` `#tests` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-23) **SHIPPED 2026-08-22 — merged `17931fc0` (branch fix/roadmap-easy-knockouts; plan: `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-22-roadmap-easy-knockout-batch.md`)**
  Found in M2 final review: the native resume path's split-refusal branch (the three distinct cwd-resolution refusal messages, see `docs/conversations.md` → "Native provider participation") has no IPC-harness test exercising it directly — it existed before M2 and M2 didn't add coverage. `session-meta-parity.test.ts` (M2, real-store IPC harness) now makes a direct test possible where none existed before; write one as a follow-up rather than relying on incidental coverage.
- [x] `sync-warning-self-clear.test.ts` "skips the file write when nothing changed" flakes — **NOT macOS-only** `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-08-12, found while re-verifying the debounce entry above; escalated 2026-08-13; **FIXED 2026-08-15 — youcoded PR #317, merge `c4fdc19c`**)
  **Root cause (2026-08-15), and it was a real production bug wearing a test flake's clothes:** `atomicWrite` in `sync-state.ts` named its temp file `<target>.tmp.<pid>` — the SAME name for every write in the process. Two overlapping writes to `.sync-warnings.json` both wrote that one temp path; the first `rename` moved it away and the second threw ENOENT. The overlap in the suite was the previous test's fake-timer-fired 60 s health check still doing real fs I/O after `stop()` (which only clears the interval); in the app the same collision is reachable when the 60 s health check overlaps a push-failure warning write. Fix: `<target>.tmp.<pid>.<seq>` (a per-call counter), and the leaking test now awaits its in-flight checks after `stop()`. 25/25 in a local loop; green on all three OSes in the PR run.
  Three consecutive macOS reds on master 2026-08-10/11 (Desktop CI runs 31526973244, 31531850229, 31539413917) are all this one case — `runHealthCheck — resolved warnings clear themselves > skips the file write when nothing changed` — with 404 other files passing. The test guards the PR #292 "rewrite only when the set actually changes" behavior.
  **2026-08-13 — reproduced on LINUX, locally, on unmodified master: 1 failure in 6 consecutive single-file runs.** That kills the "macOS-hostile clock/fs assumptions" hypothesis this entry previously steered toward — it is platform-independent and just rarer elsewhere. The failure is concrete, not a timeout: `Error: ENOENT: no such file or directory, rename '/tmp/sync-warning-self-clear-<pid>-<ts>/.claude/.sync-warnings.json.tmp.<pid>' -> '.../.sync-warnings.json'` thrown from `atomicWrite` (`src/main/sync-state.ts:226`) via `writeWarnings` (`:366`) ← `SyncService.runHealthCheck` (`sync-service.ts:1233`). So the tmp file is gone by the time `rename` runs, or its directory is — suspect the test's own cleanup racing a still-running periodic health check (PR #292 made `runHealthCheck` re-run every 60s and the suite drives timers), not the atomic-write helper. Next step: check whether the test awaits `stop()` before removing its temp home. Found incidentally during the dismiss-ends-turn full-suite run; that branch touches nothing in sync.
- [x] `session-meta-native-refusal.test.ts` flaked locally under parallel workers (`/tmp` race) `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-23, **MOOT — the file was deleted 2026-07-22 in youcoded `c3db2742`, one day before this entry was filed; its successor `session-meta-parity.test.ts` already uses per-run `mkdtempSync` dirs, the prescribed fix. Verified 2026-08-12.**)
  Seen once during git-surface work (Linux, full-suite parallel run): failed on first run, clean on rerun and standalone — implementer diagnosed a pre-existing `/tmp` path race between vitest workers. One strike; if it recurs, give the test a worker-unique temp dir (`mkdtemp`) like the git fixtures use.
- [x] `content-search.test.ts` "caps total hits and reports truncation" flaked EBUSY on Windows CI `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#artifacts` (added 2026-07-23; FIXED 2026-07-23 — recurred same-day on the same PR, so the prescribed fix shipped: youcoded PR #213 commit `4c4c57f7` gives the teardown rm `maxRetries: 5, retryDelay: 100`. #213 merged to master 2026-07-23 — CLOSED)
  Seen twice on youcoded PR #213 (runs 30030655741 + the 7d953530 lap, windows-latest — a branch that touches nothing in artifacts): teardown `rmdir` of the `ycd-search-*` temp dir failed `EBUSY: resource busy or locked` — classic Windows race where the spawned ripgrep hasn't released the dir when afterEach removes it.
- [x] `UnsavedChangesDialog` Escape also fires the app ESC cascade underneath `bug` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-22, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #298, merge `c7899ffe`**: raw listener replaced with `useEscClose(true, onCancel)`; test proves one Esc closes only the dialog, second Esc pops the layer beneath, and `defaultPrevented` still suppresses the PTY passthrough. Review confirmed the old double-fire was order-INdependent (stopPropagation can't block same-target capture listeners) and the fix also made Android hardware-back dismiss the dialog, which the raw listener never did.)
  Found while fixing the identical bug in the git-surface discard dialog (branch `feat/git-surface`, commit `b2301eb8`): the dialog registered its own raw capture-phase `keydown` listener and called `stopPropagation()`, but `EscCloseProvider` (`use-esc-close.tsx`) ALSO listens capture-phase on the same `window` target — and same-target listeners are immune to `stopPropagation()` (only `stopImmediatePropagation()` stops them). One Escape press therefore cancelled the dialog AND popped the drawer's ESC stack beneath it.
- [x] `UnsavedChangesDialog` still hand-rolls its scrim instead of the Scrim/OverlayPanel layer system `bug` `#renderer` `#ui-consistency` (added 2026-08-12, found by the PR #298 review; **FIXED same day — youcoded PR #305, merge `d686530e`**: migrated to `<Scrim layer={3}>` + `<OverlayPanel layer={3}>` mirroring DiscardConfirmDialog (deliberately no `destructive` ring — headline action is Save), AND the overlay-layer-authority guard was widened with a repo-wide red-first `bg-black/\d+` scan so the class can't recur — it found zero other offenders. **Destin: eyeball the dialog once** — backdrop went from cold black to theme-tinted `--scrim-heavy`, panel gained the theme rounding/shadow. Review parity-verified click-outside, Esc stack, and layer ordering; noted non-blocking: no focus trap (pre-existing), and the dialog is host-local stacking like its sibling.)
- [x] Git surface: conflicted (unmerged) files read as clean `bug` `#git` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-22, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #304, merge `87dd28b1`**: `u ` lines parsed into a `'conflicted'` kind (fixture captured from a real merge-conflict repo — the review independently reproduced its blob hashes), amber "Conflict" word in the footer + badge on the review card, `staged:false` matching git's own commit refusal. The review proved discard-on-unmerged is safe (was previously a silent no-op LIE — `{ok:true}` for a file it never touched), and a follow-up hardening disables "Include in commit" on conflicted files so a non-technical user can't stage conflict markers. **Destin: eyeball the amber Conflict badge/footer word.** Residuals filed as their own entries: core.quotePath paths, TerminalRightSlot keying, rewrite staleness.)

- [x] Git surface: "Show more" pagination vs `git:changed` refresh can drop/duplicate log entries `bug` `#git` (added 2026-07-22, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — youcoded PR #304, merge `87dd28b1`. Fixed by dedupe-by-sha, NOT the clear-on-refresh this entry sketched**: refresh rides the view's own stage/unstage `git:changed` events, so clearing would collapse the user's opened history on every "Include in commit" tick; dedupe (page-one wins, `--skip` from the deduped count) kills both the duplicate-React-key and skip-gap failure modes. Known bounded residual on history REWRITE mid-pagination — own entry above.)

Chat view is fully safe (per-session keyed drawers); the terminal-view unkeyed-drawer cousin is its own entry above.)
- [x] Halftone Dimension's bubble `custom_css` targets selectors that don't exist `bug` `#themes` (added 2026-07-20; FIXED 2026-07-22 — wecoded-themes #20 (merged) fixes the pack, youcoded #197 (merged `694fc94b`) adds `lintCustomCssSelectors()` so an inert selector warns instead of shipping silently. Confirmed halftone is the ONLY offender: the other six packs use no bubble `custom_css` at all. The `:nth-child(odd)` cyan alternation was DROPPED, not renamed — `.assistant-bubble` is the only child of its flex wrapper, so `:nth-child(odd)` always matches and a straight rename would have painted every assistant bubble cyan)
  `wecoded-themes/themes/halftone-dimension/manifest.json`'s `custom_css` styles `.chat-bubble.user` and `.chat-bubble.assistant` — the live renderer uses **`.user-bubble`** (`UserMessage.tsx:72`) and **`.assistant-bubble`** (`AssistantTurnBubble.tsx:362`). So the pack's user-bubble glow, its 1.5px accent border, and the assistant border-left (incl. the `:nth-child(odd)` cyan alternation) are **all dead in the shipping app** — the theme renders without the bubble treatment its previews imply. Fix is a one-line selector rename in the manifest, but check the other six community themes for the same mistake before shipping it, and confirm against the bubble-style variants at `globals.css:1051,1060,1066` which already hook the real class names. Note the pack also has an internal inconsistency worth fixing in the same pass: token `accent` is `#E51F48` while every `custom_css` glow hardcodes `#e8234a`. (found 2026-07-20 while building the session-switch animation mockup, which reproduced the pack faithfully and surfaced that the rules never applied)
- [x] Remote connect crashed the whole screen on a phone — "undefined is not a function (near '…theme of themeEntries…')" `bug` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-20 — youcoded merge `492ff880`)
  Regression from `f17d00cb`. That merge made unbridged channels answer `{ok:false,unsupported:true}` instead of dropping; the shim then RESOLVED with that payload ("so nothing crashes"), which was backwards — callers expect the channel's real return *shape*. `marketplace-context.tsx:172` does `theme.marketplace.list().catch(() => [])`; the `.catch` never fired, the object survived `themes || []`, and `for (const theme of themeEntries)` threw, blanking the screen. Fix: shim REJECTS unsupported channels (restores the contract every `.catch(() => [])` was written against — they used to reject on the 30 s timeout; now they reject in ms), plus `Array.isArray` guards on all five list setters in marketplace-context. The prior test literally asserted "still resolves … so the caller does not crash" — it encoded the bug; replaced. Found because Destin pushed back on a wrong "production-bundle" hypothesis, and switching the dev server back to Vite un-minified `themeEntries`.
- [x] Remote logging could crash the main process (EPIPE) + warn-spam on unbridged channels `bug` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-20 — youcoded merge `9c31808e`)
  A closed stdout/stderr throws EPIPE; with no `error` listener that's an uncaught exception that kills the main process — the app died over a log line ("A JavaScript error occurred in the main process / write EPIPE"). Guarded in `main.ts` at import time (reproduced through the exact `2>&1 | head` pipeline: unguarded exits 1, guarded exits 0). Second bug compounding it: the `default:` case warned on EVERY unhandled request, and `useAttentionClassifier` polls the unbridged `terminal:get-screen-text` once a second → a log line per second. Now deduped per channel. Both pinned in `tests/remote-server.test.ts`. (Surfaced by a crash that only happened because a dev launch was mistakenly piped into `head` — the EPIPE path was latent in production regardless.)
- [x] `artifact-store.test.ts` "retries on CAS conflict up to MAX_RETRIES" is flaky `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-20; FIXED 2026-07-22, youcoded PR #198 `79ac5802` — **it was NOT flake: a real data-loss bug in the CAS retry path**, fixed in `artifact-store.ts` (+43) and mirrored in Android `ArtifactStore.kt`. The suspicion recorded here was right — an intermittent failure in a compare-and-swap path was losing one of three concurrent appends, not merely failing to retry. Independently re-confirmed during the 2026-07-22 theme-layer consequence sweep, where it reproduced 5/5 locally on clean master while CI stayed green — i.e. environment-dependent, which is why the suite had tolerated it)
  Fails roughly 1 in 3 full-suite runs with `expected length 3 but got 2` at `tests/artifacts/artifact-store.test.ts:116` — the third CAS retry doesn't land. **Confirmed pre-existing**, not caused by the Tier 1 remote-perf merge: reproduced 3/8 runs on `4cc745ec`, which predates it. A flaky test in the suite is corrosive — it trains sessions to re-run until green, which is exactly how a real regression gets waved through. Likely a timing/mtime-resolution assumption in the retry loop rather than a genuine race in `appendVersion`. (found 2026-07-20 verifying the Tier 1 merge)
  **Mechanism (PR #198):** `updatedAt` is the CAS comparand and has millisecond resolution, so two writers inside the SAME millisecond left the on-disk token byte-identical to the value the other writer had already read — its CAS then passed on a value that had changed underneath it (classic ABA) and its stale copy clobbered the first record. Both callers got `committed: true` and one artifact silently vanished. Measured **149/300** same-millisecond pairs. That is why the failing assertion is `toHaveLength(3)` — a LOST RECORD, not the "third CAS retry doesn't land" this entry originally guessed — and why it fails MORE on a fast idle machine and LESS under load, the opposite of the fixed-budget family it was filed alongside. Fix: `writeSidecar` now advances `updatedAt` past the token the writer read, so every commit strictly increases it and a stale reader can never match; placed there so all five sidecar writers inherit it, and mirrored in `app/.../ArtifactStore.kt`, which shares the CAS model and writes the same file. Pinned by a 60-iteration concurrency loop + a direct strictly-advances assertion (both verified red with the fix reverted). Creation-path sibling still open, see the `#artifacts` entry above.
- [x] Enabling remote access in Settings does nothing until the app restarts `bug` `#remote` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-20 — youcoded merge `756ee273`)
  `REMOTE_SET_CONFIG` (`ipc-handlers.ts:1311-1320`) sets `remoteConfig.enabled = true` and saves, but never starts the server — `remoteServer.start()` runs exactly once at boot (`main.ts:1325`), when `enabled` was still false. So a user flips the toggle, the UI shows it on, and nothing is listening; the browser gets ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED with no hint that a restart is required. Affects the built app, not just dev. Fix is either starting/stopping the server from the handler, or telling the user a restart is needed — the current silent no-op is the worst of both. (found 2026-07-20 while trying to reach the remote-hydration runtime checkpoint)
  **Fixed 2026-07-20** (`756ee273`): the handler starts on enable and stops on disable. Making `start()` runtime-callable exposed four states unreachable when it only ran at boot, all fixed in the same change — non-idempotent re-subscription of the SessionManager/HookRelay listeners, `listen()` errors with no handler at all (the promise never settled; the new test *times out* at 5003ms against the old code rather than failing), `loadTokens()` being constructor-only while `stop()` clears the map, and an uncancelled upload-cleanup interval. Failures roll `enabled` back, persist it, and surface the real OS error under the toggle. Open follow-up: `stop()` still clears in-memory tokens, so toggling off de-pairs every device rather than pausing — pre-existing, preserved deliberately, cheap to change if that reads wrong.
- [x] Sync reports green "All synced" on a device that has never synced once `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-22 — youcoded merge `efab7e7d`)
  `SyncPanel.tsx:894` derives status as a fallback ladder — `enabling → !enabled → errorMsg → spacesSyncing → 'synced'`. The green `'synced'` state is the bare `else`: it asserts only "enabled, nothing erroring right now, nothing in flight", never that a push or pull has **ever** succeeded. A device that has never contacted the remote lands on green. Observed 2026-07-20 on a fresh Intel macOS VM running `1.3.0-beta.8`: panel read `All synced · GitHub · instant sync on · 1 Device`, while `youcoded-sync-personal` held 3110 blobs, 2 device records (`destinsZ13` linux, `GalaxyBook` win32 — the VM absent), 1 `ProjectSync/` entry and 3074 conversations. Every commit that day was authored by `destinsZ13`; the VM had neither pushed nor pulled. Root cause of the *sync* failure was mundane — `gh` isn't installed on a stock macOS (Xcode CLT ships `git`, not `gh`) — but the reporting failure is the bug: **`space-manager.ts:53` already throws the correct plain-language `'GitHub CLI (gh) is not installed — sync needs it to create your private repos'`, and it never reached the UI.** Per `docs/sync-spaces.md`, enable-path provisioning failures don't reject `enable()`; they arrive as broadcast error events, so that channel is where it was lost — determine whether the event is never emitted, emitted before the panel mounts and dropped from `recentEvents`, or emitted and then superseded. Compounding the false confidence: `instant sync on` came from the SyncHub socket, which authenticates against the account and needs no `gh` at all, so the one subsystem that *could* connect did. Two fixes, both needed: (1) gate green on evidence of a completed sync (e.g. `lastSyncEpoch`/per-space success) and give never-synced-yet its own non-green state, and (2) make the `gh`-missing error survive to the panel. Directly contradicts docs/error-message-standards.md — a status that says "synced" when nothing has ever synced is the misleading-message failure mode applied to success rather than error. Related: `#32` (misleading error messages audit). (found 2026-07-20 dogfooding beta.8 on the macOS VM) **Update 2026-07-22:** loss mechanism confirmed = emitted-then-superseded: the no-remote space emitted a phantom `synced` ~1s after the error, which `latestUnresolvedError` treated as resolution; poll re-emitted it forever and `ensureRemote` was never retried. **Fixed 2026-07-22** (youcoded merge `efab7e7d`): remote-less spaces never emit `synced`; provisioning retries every cycle via `ensureProvisioned` (self-healing, error re-emitted verbatim until cured); green is evidence-gated via pure `deriveSyncBoxState` (all active spaces provisioned + Personal `lastSyncAt` non-null, persisted in `sync-spaces.json`); enable preflights `github:status` into the connect modal and resumes after connect. Plan: docs/archive/plans/2026-07-22-sync-setup-overhaul.md (Phase 1 — shipped).
- [x] First-sync hydration has no progress state — "Setting up…" names the wrong operation and promises the wrong duration `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-20, fixed 2026-07-22 — youcoded merge `efab7e7d`; the "minimum viable" distinct hydrating phase shipped — real percentages via `git fetch --progress` deferred to overhaul Phase 2+)
  Enabling sync on a new device shows exactly one state for the entire first sync: `Setting up… / Creating your private repositories — a few seconds.` (`SyncPanel.tsx:905,929`). Both halves are wrong for the step that actually takes the time. Repo provisioning IS a few seconds; the **pull that follows** is the long pole — on Destin's account that's 3110 blobs / 3074 conversations over a fresh unrelated-histories merge, and it is completely unnarrated. The user watches a card claiming "a few seconds" for minutes and reasonably concludes it has stalled (reported verbatim 2026-07-20: *"'setting up' just makes it feel like it stalled"*). **This is not a copy fix — the data does not exist.** `enabling` is a local `useState` boolean in the panel (`SyncPanel.tsx:432`) flipped around the `enable()` call, so it tracks "we called enable", not progress; and `SpaceSyncEvent` (`sync-spaces/types.ts`) has no progress-shaped variant at all — `synced` carries only `{pushed, updated}` booleans, with no file/byte counts or phase. Doing this properly means emitting phase + progress from the engine/transport (git pull is the only place the counts exist) and adding a `hydrating` state to the ladder, which is the same ladder that needs a never-synced-yet state per the entry above — **do these two together, they are one state-machine fix.** Also seen in the same screenshot: three contradictory surfaces at once — sidebar `Backup & Sync · Not configured`, panel `Setting up…`, and a red `No sync configured / Set up sync` warning card — which is the same root cause (status derived from disconnected local flags rather than one source of truth). Minimum viable version if the full progress plumbing is too big: a distinct "Downloading your synced data — this can take a few minutes on first sync" phase entered once provisioning returns, even without a percentage. (found 2026-07-20 dogfooding beta.8 on the macOS VM) **Update 2026-07-22:** folded into the one-state-machine fix with the entry above. Plan: docs/archive/plans/2026-07-22-sync-setup-overhaul.md (Phase 1).

- [x] Tighten `PRESENCE_STALE_MS` once the Android app-level ping release is widespread `task` `#social` `#worker` (added 2026-07-22, done 2026-07-22 — wecoded-marketplace PR #55)

Accepted cost at beta scale: Android builds without the app-level ping (unshipped in any APK) flap offline/online ~every 10–15 min until they update — the v1.3 APK carries the ping. Android Doze deferring pings on an idle phone reads as "away" and is treated as correct behavior.
- [x] Desktop vitest flake: `ipc-handlers.test.ts` import-race on shared `/tmp/youcoded-vitest-home` `bug` `#tests` (added 2026-07-23, **FIXED 2026-08-05 — youcoded `f05b2711`: `SkillConfigStore.save()` now writes a per-writer temp name (`.${pid}.${seq}.tmp`) before the rename, so the exact ENOENT (`youcoded-skills.json.tmp` → `youcoded-skills.json`) can no longer collide. Verified on master 2026-08-12.** Note neither roadmap-proposed fix was taken — the shared `TEST_HOME` in `vitest.config.ts:30` is still one dir for all workers, so the BROADER shared-temp-home class lives on as the separate "Pre-existing vitest flake — parallel workers race on the shared temp home" entry above.)
  Intermittent (~50% in a worktree, also reproduced on clean master): collection of `tests/ipc-handlers.test.ts` failed with ENOENT renaming `youcoded-skills.json.tmp` → `youcoded-skills.json` under `/tmp/youcoded-vitest-home`. Import of `main.ts` triggers `SkillConfigStore.migrate → save` as a module side effect, and parallel vitest workers share the same temp HOME, so two files importing main-process modules raced the atomic-rename.

Verified 2026-08-12.**)
