- [x] Dependabot rollout across the workspace `task` `#ci` `#tooling` (added 2026-07-23, **DONE 2026-07-23**)
  Weekly grouped dependency-update PRs across youcoded (npm desktop + partykit, Gradle, GitHub Actions), wecoded-marketplace (worker npm + Actions), and wecoded-themes (npm + Actions). **Part A closed two CI gaps first:** youcoded `desktop-ci`/`android-ci` now check every PR via `pull_request` (was a branch-name allowlist that `dependabot/**` matched none of), and marketplace gained `worker-ci.yml` — worker code previously had NO pre-merge check (tests ran only inside the post-merge deploy job, against production), and it caught **three breaking bumps on day one**. **Tiered ignore policy:** `node-pty` ignored entirely (its post-install patch exits 0 when its target line is gone → silently drops the macOS spawn-helper fix with green CI), `electron`/`koffi`/`@vscode/ripgrep` majors ignored (green tests don't prove a *packaged* build works); no auto-merge or branch protection. PRs merged 2026-07-23: youcoded #216 + #217 + #238, marketplace #56 + #57, themes #21. **V1–V5 all verified**, including V1 — the one assumption unverifiable before the first run: Dependabot's `dependabot/**` PRs DO get a check rollup. Dependabot auto-ran on merge (29 PRs; 12 bumps merged, 5 held as genuine major breakage). **Amendment #238:** the design's two Gradle entries duplicated every app dependency (#235 == #222 — same dep, same file) because Dependabot resolves the whole multi-project build from `/`; the `/app` entry was deleted. **Unanticipated finding — the desktop test flake now taxes this workflow:** it produced 4 of 8 non-superseded youcoded false reds in the first batch, so a red youcoded Dependabot PR does NOT imply a bad bump. Triage rule worth keeping: the failing job name names the surface — bare `build` = Android/Gradle CI (real dependency breakage), `build (windows-latest)`/`build (macos-latest)` = desktop matrix (suspect the flake, especially when the dep can't touch that surface). Fixing that flake (the two `#tests` Bugs entries above) is the highest-value follow-up — until then each weekly batch needs manual re-runs to read true status, the exact effort this automation exists to remove. Retro: **`docs/archive/specs/2026-07-23-dependabot-design.md` §8**; plan `docs/archive/plans/2026-07-23-dependabot-implementation.md`.
- [x] Native-session takeover destroys the holder and transfers nothing `bug` `#native-runtime` `#sync` (added 2026-07-18; SHIPPED 2026-07-19 — youcoded#177, merge `fe8529ba`; verified against master + closed 2026-07-22)
  Fixed by #177: single-writer orphan guard in resume()/create(), awaited destroyNative in takeover + a session-exit backstop, native lease acquire reverted (takeover unreachable for native until parity re-enables it), phantom-record gate incl. remote-server. Residue (making the lease meaningful again) SHIPPED via M2 — native lease re-enabled + real takeover quiesce (youcoded #212, merge 60d56a67; plan archived at docs/archive/plans/2026-07-22-m2-conversations-sync-plan.md). Investigation: docs/archive/investigations/2026-07-18-native-session-takeover-gap.md (archived shipped 2026-07-23).
- [x] `subagent-watcher` fs.watch test flakes on macOS, failing every desktop build `bug` `#tests` `#ci` (added 2026-07-16, confirmed closed 2026-07-19)
  `desktop/tests/subagent-watcher.test.ts:90` ("picks up a subagent file that appears after start") does `await wait(1500) // allow fs.watch/poll to fire` then asserts the emitted event — on the macOS runner nothing arrives in time, so `stamped?.data.parentAgentToolUseId` is `undefined` and `npm test` exits 1 (`1 failed | 2343 passed`). Observed on beta run 29553208961 (2026-07-16); Linux + Windows passed the same suite in the same run, and the sibling `replays an existing subagent file on start` passes because it uses the replay path and never arms the watcher. macOS `fs.watch` goes through FSEvents, whose coalescing latency exceeds the fixed 1500 ms budget under runner load. Impact is wider than one red check: `npm test` gates the Build step, so the macOS `.dmg` never gets produced — a beta dispatch yields no macOS artifact, and the whole run reports failure even when Win/Linux are fine. Fix by replacing the fixed sleep with polling until the condition holds (bounded retry) rather than raising the constant; the timeout is the symptom, the fixed-budget assumption is the bug. Not yet confirmed whether it's a hard macOS failure or load-dependent flake — one observation so far, worth a re-dispatch to tell. **Update 2026-07-16 — the file is NOT macOS-only-fragile: it flakes on Windows too.** A full-suite `npm test` on an UNMODIFIED `origin/master` worktree (verifying youcoded#161) failed `subagent-watcher > live-path flush: buffered subagent events emit in order after parent arrives` on Windows; it passed in isolation immediately after. Note that's a DIFFERENT test case than the line-90 one above, so this does NOT settle whether the macOS failure is hard or load-dependent — but it does show the whole file's timing assumptions break under parallel load on a second platform, which strengthens the fixed-budget diagnosis and the polling fix. Same family as the git-heavy sync entry below; the two are plausibly one fix (bounded-retry polling + a saner pool/timeout for load-sensitive tests). **Update 2026-07-17 — FIX SHIPPED (youcoded PR #163, merge `9138262`), but deliberately left OPEN: macOS is still UNCONFIRMED.** Every fixed sleep before a positive assertion in this file is now `vi.waitFor(...)` bounded-retry polling — including line 90's `wait(1500)`, the exact case that failed the macOS build — plus two hand-rolled `20x50ms` loops (a 1s ceiling for fs.watch delivery was the same losing bet). Polling has no fixed budget, so FSEvents coalescing latency cannot blow it; that's the reasoning, NOT an observation. **Why it can't be closed yet: `desktop-ci.yml` runs `ubuntu-latest` ONLY** — no PR check has ever executed this file on macOS. Only `desktop-release.yml` / `desktop-test-build.yml` run the macos-latest matrix, which is why this only ever surfaced on a beta dispatch. **Close this when the next desktop test-build/release goes green on macOS; if the `.dmg` job is still red, reopen with the runner log** — the remaining suspects are the Node-side sleeps this fix did NOT touch (the ones guarding NEGATIVE assertions, kept on purpose: there's no condition to poll for and a short sleep there fails safe). **CLOSED 2026-07-19 — the stated close condition is met.** The `macos-latest` leg (which runs `npm test` before Build, so a green macOS job means this file passed) went green on three independent test-build dispatches this session: runs 29678071273, 29680002436, and 29701441150 — the last two on branches whose only deltas were Windows path handling, untouched by `fs.watch` timing. The `vi.waitFor` bounded-retry fix from PR #163 holds on FSEvents. Note the Windows leg of run 29678071273 DID fail, but on an unrelated 8.3-short-name bug (see the two Windows entries under ## Shipped), not on this file.
- [x] Git-heavy sync tests time out at 30s under full-suite parallel load (Windows) `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-07-16, shipped 2026-07-17)
  Observed across three consecutive `npm test` runs in a Windows worktree while verifying the device-identity work: the failing set VARIES run to run (run 1: `sync-spaces-project-discovery` + an unhandled "Worker exited unexpectedly"; run 2: `sync-spaces-project-discovery` alone; run 3: `sync-spaces-project-discovery` **and** `sync-spaces-git-transport > divergent edits converge`), and pass counts drift with it (2374 / 2382 / 2381 of 2418). Every failure is `Error: Test timed out in 30000ms`; run 3 also threw `EPERM, Permission denied` from `tests/sync-spaces-git-transport.test.ts:29` `fs.rmSync(tmp, …)` cleaning its temp dir — Windows handle contention. Both tests pass in ISOLATION (project-discovery: 19.6s in the worktree, 27.3s in the main checkout — i.e. already 65–90% of the 30s budget before any parallel load). Root cause is the fixed-budget assumption, same family as the `subagent-watcher` entry above: these spawn real `git` via `execFileSync`, so their wall-clock scales with machine load, and vitest's parallel pool guarantees load. Not caused by the device-identity change — neither test imports `device-identity`/`device-registry` (grep: 0 hits). Fix candidates: give the git-integration tests an explicit generous `testTimeout` (they are integration, not unit), and/or run them in a non-parallel pool/shard; retry-on-EPERM for the Windows temp cleanup. Worth confirming whether CI (Linux/macOS runners) hits the same ceiling — `npm test` gates the Build step, so a load-dependent 30s timeout can nondeterministically block a release build. **Update 2026-07-16 — the blast radius is wider than the two git tests.** A fourth run on a heavily-loaded machine failed 5 tests across 4 files: both git tests above, TWO `subagent-watcher` cases, and `transcript-watcher > deduplicates events by uuid` (a file neither entry named until now). All four files then passed together in ISOLATION, 55/55, on the same branch — so the whole `fs.watch`/subprocess-timing family degrades together as machine load rises, and the observed victim set is a function of load, not of any one test. Practical read for whoever picks this up: don't chase individual tests: treat it as one fix (bounded-retry polling instead of fixed sleeps + a generous explicit `testTimeout` or a serial pool for the integration tests), and don't trust a single red run to identify the culprit. **SHIPPED 2026-07-17 — youcoded PR #163 (merge `9138262`), as the single family fix this entry called for.** The 30s ceilings went to 120s and the EPERM cleanup now uses `maxRetries`. **The trap that cost a cycle:** raising the file-level `vi.setConfig` ALONE DID NOT WORK — three separate things pinned 30s, and an inline `}, 30_000)` **third argument on `it()` silently overrides `vi.setConfig`**; so does the shared `sync-transport-contract.ts`'s own `vi.setConfig(30_000)`, which runs when `describeTransportContract()` is CALLED and clobbers the calling file's config. The inline args are gone so each file has ONE documented knob — don't reintroduce them. Verified on Windows: BEFORE 3/3 full-suite runs failed; AFTER 7 runs, 6 fully clean (counts reconciling), `tsc` clean, Linux CI green. The `subagent-watcher` entry above carries the remaining macOS confirmation.
- [x] `sync-warnings-lifecycle` test flakes under full-suite load — module-load-order HOME freeze `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-07-17)
  **SHIPPED 2026-07-22 — youcoded PR #198 (merge `79ac5802`). The stated root cause was wrong.** It was never module-load ORDER. The suite never redirected HOME at all: nothing assigned it, and the static `import` is hoisted above any assignment that might have tried — its `tmpHome`/`claudeDir`/`warningsPath` consts were dead weight. So every assertion ran against the developer's REAL `~/.claude/.sync-warnings.json`, which `writeWarnings([])` DELETES. The failing case asserts no `OFFLINE` warning survives, and `SyncService.runHealthCheck()` writes exactly an OFFLINE warning to exactly that path when a YouCoded instance launches (`sync-service.ts:163` → `:1042`). A second PROCESS sharing one real file, not test ordering — no other suite writes warnings at all. That also explains why it was seen locally with the app running and never on CI runners. Fixed with a test-only seam on each module that froze a real path (`setClaudeDirForTests()` in `sync-state.ts`; an optional `home` constructor arg on `SyncService`). Note `process.env.HOME` would NOT have worked: `os.homedir()` reads USERPROFILE on Windows, where this was observed.
  **Follow-on in the same PR — the whole class is gone.** `vitest.config.ts` now points HOME *and* USERPROFILE at a throwaway sandbox (wiped once per run by `tests/global-setup.ts`, not at config load — that races workers), so no test *can* reach real `~/.claude`. Prevention rather than detection, because this bug's test file never mentioned the home directory: a module three imports deep resolved it, so reviewing test files could not have caught it, and a snapshot tripwire would never be clean (the live app and Claude Code both write `~/.claude` continuously). What it exposed: the suite creates **485 files** under the fake home — `.claude.json`, `youcoded-skills.json` (favorites, chips, overrides, private prompt skills), `desktop.log`, a `.claude.json` backup, `plugins/`, `wecoded-themes/`, a 400+ file marketplace-cache clone, `.youcoded/providers.json`. Several are no-ops when state already exists, so that is the set of paths at risk, not proof all 485 were rewritten every run; on a CI runner, where the home starts empty, all were created. Pinned by `tests/home-isolation.test.ts` (verified red on all four assertions with the redirect removed).
  `desktop/tests/sync-warnings-lifecycle.test.ts > sync warning store > clearWarningsByCode removes only matching code` fails intermittently in the full parallel suite (~2 of 4 observed runs while landing the terminal-frame-panels UI work, 2026-07-17) but passes 10/10 in ISOLATION. **Distinct root cause from the fixed-budget timeout family above** — not a timeout. `sync-state.ts` computes its paths from `os.homedir()` at MODULE-LOAD time, and this suite redirects `HOME` to a tmp dir before importing it (see the file's own comment, lines 21–23); but if ANY other test file imports `sync-state.ts` first under vitest's parallel scheduling, the path is frozen to the real/other HOME and this suite then reads/writes warnings from the wrong location → order-dependent failure. NOT caused by the UI work that surfaced it (renderer-only changes; nothing in that branch imports `sync-state`). Fix: make the suite import-order-independent — inject the base dir instead of freezing it from `os.homedir()` at load, or `vi.resetModules()` + re-import after setting HOME rather than relying on being the first importer. Same "test pollution, passes isolated" smell as the timeout family, different mechanism (global module state, not a time budget).
- [x] The contrast audit silently stopped checking two pairs, hiding both bugs below `bug` `#themes` `#a11y` `#tooling` (added 2026-07-22; FIXED same day — wecoded-marketplace #51 (merged `facf695`) canonical, youcoded #197 (merged `694fc94b`) + wecoded-themes #20 (merged) re-vendor)
  youcoded PR #187 consolidated three hand-written contrast tables into the canonical theme-builder copy and **dropped `panel/canvas` and `on-destructive/destructive` in the process**. Dropping a rule makes the audit *quieter*, not louder, so nothing failed: `node desktop/scripts/audit-theme-contrast.mjs` printed "All 11 themes pass" while creme still shipped panel/canvas at 1.051 and all 11 themes still shipped a danger label at 4.21:1. **Both bug entries below cited a repro command that had silently stopped reproducing** — the next person to check them would reasonably have closed them as already-fixed. Tell that it was accidental: the theme-builder's own `reference/tokens.md` still documented the "panel vs canvas only needs 1.07:1" rule the code had lost. Fix also moves `destructive`/`on-destructive` synthesis INTO `evaluate()` — neither token is declared by most packs (one falls back to the app default, the other is derived at runtime), so auditing only declared tokens skipped every theme, and having each consumer derive its own is exactly how one of them lost the pair. **Lesson worth keeping: a consolidation that removes checks needs a before/after count of rules evaluated, because a shrinking rule set is invisible in a green run.**
- [x] Crème chrome doesn't separate from content — `panel/canvas` fails the contrast floor `bug` `#themes` `#a11y` (added 2026-07-16; FIXED 2026-07-22 — youcoded #197 (merged `694fc94b`). Destin picked `--canvas: #F6EEE1` (1.132) from rendered options. NOTE the entry below was WRONG about the lever: the fix is NOT a new `--panel` value — creme's ramp is tightly packed and panel is pinned between canvas above and inset/edge below, so darkening panel to #E8DCC8 clears this pair but BREAKS `inset vs panel` (1.183, min 1.2) and `edge on panel` (1.44, min 1.5). Lightening canvas moves every other pair the right way. `--on-accent` tracks canvas, per the convention all four built-ins hold)
  `node desktop/scripts/audit-theme-contrast.mjs` reports `✗ panel/canvas 1.048:1 (min 1.07)` for creme: panel `#EBE1D1` vs canvas `#F0E6D6` are nearly the same color, so headers/drawers/popup surfaces don't visually separate from the content behind them. Pre-existing and the ONLY remaining creme failure — the fg-muted/fg-faint half was fixed 2026-07-16 (UI-consistency change 37, youcoded `4424c7d8`), so creme still prints ❌ purely on this pair. Deliberately left out of the UI-primitives PR: fixing needs a new `--panel` value, which changes how a built-in theme actually looks and deserves a before/after render + Destin's eye rather than a silent ride-along in a primitives migration. Fix in BOTH `src/renderer/themes/builtin/creme.json` and the `[data-theme="creme"]` block in globals.css — `tests/theme-builtin-sources.test.ts` now pins the two together and will fail if only one moves. The other three built-ins pass this pair.
- [x] Default `--destructive` red can't carry an AA-contrast label at any text color `bug` `#themes` `#a11y` (added 2026-07-16; FIXED 2026-07-22 — youcoded #197 (merged `694fc94b`). Destin chose to darken the red rather than relax the audit pair: `#DD4444` → `#C62828` (5.62:1), the largest margin of the candidates. The FIXED status red `#DD4444` used for state indicators is deliberately unchanged, so the two now differ — three code comments claiming they were "identical #DD4444 today" were corrected. Pinned by a property assertion in `tests/theme-engine.test.ts` (label ≥ 4.5), not a hex, so a future repalette stays free. **CONSEQUENCE — see the `--destructive` is over-constrained entry below: darkening the red fixed the fill but degraded `text-destructive` on dark themes.**)
- [x] `--destructive` is over-constrained — one token serves two opposed jobs `bug` `#themes` `#a11y` (added 2026-07-22; FIXED same day in youcoded #197 (merged `694fc94b`) + wecoded-marketplace #51 (merged `facf695`) — token split into `--destructive` (fill) and `--destructive-fg` (text), derived per theme by `deriveDestructiveFg()`; all 10 `text-destructive` sites migrated; two new HARD audit rules cover destructive-as-text, which had NO rule before. Derived: midnight/dark `#D45C5C`, halftone `#D15353`, light `#C62828` unchanged, creme `#BE2626`)
  The token is used BOTH as a filled surface (`bg-destructive` on danger buttons/toggles, where the label needs contrast *against* it) AND as text (`text-destructive`, ~10 sites: skip-permissions warnings at `text-[10px]`, `states.tsx:164` at `text-3xs`, `danger-outline` labels, error strings — where it needs contrast *against the canvas*). Those pull in opposite directions on dark themes: a fill wants to be DARK so a white label reads; text on a dark canvas wants to be LIGHT. **Proved by sweep: ZERO reds satisfy both roles at AA (4.5) across all five themes.** At AA-large (3.0) for the text role, 495 do — but most `text-destructive` sites render at 10-12px, where AA-large does not apply. So no choice of hex fixes this; only splitting the token does. Measured for `#C62828` (text on canvas/panel): midnight 3.37/3.08, dark 3.36/3.13, halftone 3.58/3.39, light 5.02/4.67, creme 4.88/4.34 — i.e. the darkening that fixed the danger-button label (:141) moved the failure onto dark-theme warning text, where the old `#DD4444` had been passing (4.49-4.78). Not a regression *introduced* by :141 so much as the same over-constraint surfacing at the other end. Fix is the `--accent`/`--on-accent` pattern applied here: a fill token plus a separate text token derived per-theme (light themes darken it, dark themes lighten it). Note the contrast audit has NO rule for `destructive on canvas/panel` — adding one today would fail, which is why this is filed rather than bolted onto #197.
  `#DD4444` scores 4.213:1 against white and 4.131:1 against near-black — both under the 4.5 WCAG AA bar for small text, and danger button labels render at `text-xs` (12px) / `text-2xs` (11px). So every danger button in every theme ships a sub-AA label today, and no choice of label color fixes it; only darkening the red does (`#D33A3A` → 4.73:1, `#C62828` → 5.62:1, `#C0392B` → 5.44:1). Surfaced by the UI-consistency change-43 work (youcoded `4424c7d8`), which added an `on-destructive/destructive` pair to the contrast audit — that pair currently prints ✗ for all 11 themes (4 built-in + 7 community), since none override `overlay.destructive` and they all inherit the default. **Not caused by change 43** — it made a long-standing failure measurable. Note the spec's change-43 text asserts "#DD4444 vs white = ~4.7:1"; that figure is wrong (it matches `#D33A3A`), and the "white if ≥4.5, else near-black" rule it justified would have flipped every danger button to near-black at LOWER contrast — the engine ships a max-contrast pick instead (pinned in `tests/theme-engine.test.ts`). Decide: darken the default red (visible change to every danger surface across all themes — wants a render), or relax that one audit pair to 3.0 (AA-large) and accept the status quo. Audit is advisory-only (not wired to CI, despite the spec's §6 calling it a "CI contrast audit"), so nothing is blocked meanwhile.
- [x] Contrast audit only checks `canvas`, so secondary text goes invisible on raised surfaces `bug` `#themes` `#a11y` (added 2026-07-19, **shipped 2026-07-20**) — wecoded-marketplace#50 (OKLCH solver + 34-rule generated table + `--fix` authoring flow), wecoded-themes#19 (7 repalettes, `::selection` fix, vendored rules, CI blocking + drift guard), youcoded#187 (4 built-ins, `globals.css` lockstep, third-copy consolidation, 106-site `fg-faint`→`fg-muted` migration). All 11 themes now pass HARD+SURFACE glass-composited. Analysis archived under `docs/archive/investigations/` and `docs/archive/prototypes/`.
  Repro (Destin, 2026-07-19, Meadow Mist): the second line of the "Compacting conversation…" card is effectively invisible — screenshot shows pale green on pale green. Cause is the ruleset, not the theme. `CompactingCard.tsx:27` is `text-xs text-fg-muted` on a `bg-inset` bubble; Meadow Mist has `fg-muted #7C9384` on `inset #BAD0B6` = **2.01:1**. It passes CI because `wecoded-themes/scripts/audit-contrast.mjs` measures all five fg tokens against **`canvas` only** (`fg-muted/canvas` = 3.13, just over its 3.0 gate) and spot-checks `inset` with a single pair (`fg/inset`). `canvas` is the *lightest* surface in a light theme, so auditing against it alone measures the best case and ships the worst — full Meadow Mist matrix: on panel `fg-muted`=2.63 `fg-faint`=1.62; on inset 2.01 / 1.24; on well 2.25 / 1.39. Three notes: (1) not one card — `text-fg-muted`/`text-fg-faint` on a non-canvas surface is a common renderer pairing, this is just where it got visible; (2) glass makes it worse and the audit can't see it — Meadow Mist is a wallpaper theme at `panels-opacity: 0.58`, so the real backdrop is `inset` mixed toward a photo and the true ratio is below the flat-token 2.01; (3) any widened matrix will fail already-published themes, so measure the existing set BEFORE picking gates or the rule gets written to whatever happens to pass. Two directions, not exclusive: widen the matrix (every fg token × every surface it can land on, with a grandfather list or version ramp), or constrain the pairing instead (`fg-muted`/`fg-faint` become canvas-only tokens; secondary text on a raised surface must be `fg-dim` or better — cheaper for theme authors, needs a guard or it decays). Patching `CompactingCard` to `text-fg-dim` is a one-line stopgap but hides the class of bug — land it WITH the rule change, not instead of it. **Second report same day (Settings → Appearance row descriptions invisible) made this much bigger — see spec §12.3.** That one is `SettingsRow.tsx:34` (`text-[10px] text-fg-muted` on the `bg-inset/50` row at `:28`): one line, **18 call sites**. A renderer sweep then showed most of the app isn't on canvas at all — `.layer-surface` (`globals.css:811`) paints `--panel` under **55 `<OverlayPanel>` usages across 45 files**, `.settings-drawer` (`:648`) under all of Settings, and `AssistantTurnBubble.tsx:362` paints `bg-inset` under every assistant message and ToolCard. Measured across all 11 shipped themes (4 built-in + 7 community): `fg-muted`/`inset` is under 3.0 in **9 of 11**; `fg-faint` on `panel` or `inset` is under 2.4 in **11 of 11** (best 2.31 Strawberry Kitty, worst 1.24 Meadow Mist). **`fg-faint` on a raised surface has never worked in any theme that has ever shipped** — an app pairing no theme can satisfy, since its 1.8-vs-canvas gate assumed decorative use. It isn't always decorative: `StatusBar.tsx:756–1017` has ~15 `bg-panel` pills whose entire content is 10px `text-fg-faint` (`In:`/`Out:`/`Cached:`/`Speed:`, and at `:914`/`:863` the strings "No changes" and "N/A"); `ThemeScreen.tsx:377/433/481/486` puts `text-fg-faint` on the SAME element as `bg-inset`; `SettingsPanel.tsx:1301` runs 9px faint permission descriptions. On the muted tier: `UsageCard.tsx:85–166` (every cost/token label), `ResumeBrowser.tsx:463–549` (all field labels), `SkillCard.tsx:141/205`, `ToolBody.tsx` (~54 occurrences). `ui/states.tsx:119` is the same bug in a shared primitive with zero call sites — latent, fix before adoption. **This kills the widen-the-matrix option** (a gate that fails 11/11 themes isn't a rule) and favours constraining the pairing: make `fg-muted`/`fg-faint` canvas-only, require `fg-dim`+ for substantive text on raised surfaces. Cheap mechanism to evaluate: `.layer-surface` already precedents descendant overrides at `globals.css:827` (the protection cascade), so `.layer-surface .text-fg-muted { color: var(--fg-dim) }` could cover all 55 overlay sites with no component churn — UNVERIFIED, must respect the same layered-vs-unlayered ordering trap, and needs sibling rules for `.settings-drawer` + the bubble. Two things it wouldn't solve: genuinely decorative faint text (separators, timestamps, placeholders) gets lifted too unless the token splits, and glass still isn't modelled (all figures are flat-token; wallpaper themes composite toward a photo and are worse). Fix order: `SettingsRow.tsx:34` → `ui/states.tsx:119` → the descendant rule → StatusBar's faint pills. Full analysis + both contrast matrices: **spec §12** (`docs/archive/specs/2026-07-16-ui-consistency-design-spec.md`). Split across repos: app owns the pairing fix (+ a `react-renderer` rule), wecoded-themes owns the audit. Not part of the UI-consistency tranches; shouldn't block them.
- [x] Linux download page still hands everyone the AppImage (FUSE step) once v1.3 ships native packages `bug` `#docs` `#install` (added 2026-07-16, shipped 2026-07-16)
  `youcoded/docs/index.html` matchers hardcode `'dl-linux': /\.AppImage$/`, so when v1.3 ships the deb/rpm/pacman targets added in youcoded#98 (2026-05-20 — postdates the v1.2.4 tag, which is why v1.2.4 offers only the AppImage), every Linux user is still handed the AppImage and its `sudo apt install libfuse2` step. **Verified in a clean Ubuntu 24.04 VM 2026-07-16:** the shipped AppImage dies with `dlopen(): error loading libfuse.so.2` on stock 24.04 (no libfuse2), while the locally-built `.deb` declares **zero** fuse deps, `apt install ./youcoded_*.deb` resolves everything itself, installs to `/opt/YouCoded`, and registers a real menu entry the AppImage never provides. Fix: offer deb → Debian/Ubuntu/Mint, rpm → Fedora, pacman → Arch, AppImage as any-distro fallback; keep the FUSE note only on the AppImage path (its `sudo apt install libfuse2` text is CORRECT — verified: libfuse2 no longer exists on 24.04 but apt resolves it to libfuse2t64 via Provides; don't "fix" it). Also un-blocks testing those three artifacts, which no release has ever shipped. See docs/vm-testing.md. **SHIPPED 2026-07-16 — youcoded PR #153 (merge `e2285d54`), live on the Pages site (verified by fetching the deployed URL).** The Linux modal now asks the distro once and hands over deb/rpm/pacman, with the FUSE note scoped to the AppImage variant alone. Options are built from the assets each release actually carries, so today's page is unchanged (v1.2.4 has no native packages) and the picker lights up on its own when v1.3 ships them — no follow-up change needed.

The colour/token half is deleted and replaced with a pointer table to the files that are the real spec; the mascot-rig + scene-companion sections are KEPT — re-verified 2026-07-22, every file and type they cite still exists and that content lives nowhere else in this repo. Did not wait on the UI-consistency spec §8 token reference, which is still unapproved and unwritten)
  Still says "DestinCode", names the wrong localStorage key (`destincode-theme`; actual `youcoded-theme`), and instructs the pre-engine workflow ([data-theme] CSS blocks + ThemeName union + DARK_THEMES array) that no longer matches the JSON + theme-engine system. Rewrite against theme-engine.ts/theme-context.tsx — or retire it into the ui-primitives/token reference doc proposed in the UI-consistency spec §8. Found during the 2026-07-16 token audit.
- [x] Ink prompt title/description hijacked by "trust" substring collision `bug` `#renderer` `#terminal-parser` (added 2026-07-16; fixed 2026-07-16, youcoded PR #157 — trust anchor re-keyed to the security-note phrase 'files you trust', TrustGate exact-matches TRUST_PROMPT_TITLE, safeguard prompt got its own 'Message Flagged' anchor + SETUP entry; 'dark mode'/'login method' keys tightened the same way; Android parser mirrored incl. nearby-lines scan window)
  Repro: Fable 5 model-switch safeguard prompt renders with the "Trust This Folder?" title and hardcoded "Claude needs your permission before working in this directory." body, but the real buttons ("Switch to Opus 4.8" / "Edit prompt and retry with Fable 5"). Root cause is two independent bare substring matches on the word "trust": (1) `ink-select-parser.ts` `TITLE_OVERRIDES['trust']` — `extractTitle()` scans the raw terminal text above ANY Ink menu for the substring `"trust"` and force-labels it `'Trust This Folder?'`; the Fable safeguard prompt's body text apparently contains "trust" somewhere, so it collides. (2) `TrustGate.tsx` `findTrustPrompt()` independently does `title.includes('trust')`, so once (1) mislabels the title, TrustGate takes over rendering and swaps in its own static copy while keeping the real (correctly-parsed) buttons array. Fix needs a non-substring anchor for both matches (e.g. an exact-title check, or a more specific keyword phrase unlikely to appear in other prompts' body text) — any future Ink prompt whose text happens to contain "trust" will hit this same bug. Screenshot on file from Destin, 2026-07-16.
- [x] Stale "answer the prompt first" toast permanently blocks chat send `bug` `#renderer` `#terminal-parser` (added 2026-07-16; #157 was a PARTIAL fix — real recurring cause found + FIXED 2026-07-17, youcoded PR #165 merge `49bcf49b`: exclude the `_history_expand` marker from the gate (shared `HISTORY_EXPAND_PROMPT_ID` const) + re-check the menu is still on screen before the detector's show-timer fires. Pinned by pty-input-gate.test.ts + use-prompt-detector.test.tsx. See correction at end for the full diagnosis + the three lower-severity cousins still open. Original #157 note: fixed 2026-07-16, youcoded PR #157 — on menu-id change usePromptDetector now cancels the pending show timer and dismisses the previously SHOWN prompt before the SETUP_PROMPT_TITLES gate can bail; gated on shownPromptRef so streaming-list id churn dispatches nothing. The "Send Anyway" override affordance moved to its own Features entry below)
  Repro: resume one or more sessions (ResumeBrowser → `session-manager.ts` launches `claude --resume <id>`, which shows CC's own "Resuming from a summary" Ink prompt — `SETUP_PROMPT_TITLES` has 'Resume Session'). If the underlying PTY screen advances to any OTHER Ink-parseable content (a permission Yes/No/Always-Allow menu, or even a false-positive numbered list in Claude's plain-text output) before the user clicks the Resume Session card, the card gets permanently stuck with `completed: false` and `InputBar.tsx`/`App.tsx` refuse all future sends with "Claude is waiting for your response — answer the prompt first," even though nothing is live on screen. Destin hit this on multiple sessions resumed after a crash — consistent with the mechanism, since every resumed session independently replays the same Resume-Session-card → next-PTY-state sequence. Root cause: `usePromptDetector.ts` lines 117-123 — `lastMenuRef.current.set(sid, menu.id)` runs BEFORE the `SETUP_PROMPT_TITLES` check, so when a new (unrecognized) menu is detected, the ref is overwritten to point at it and the function returns early without dismissing the OLD prompt. Dismissal (`DISMISS_PROMPT`, the `else if (lastMenuId)` branch) only ever fires for whatever id `lastMenuRef` *currently* holds, so the orphaned old entry can never be cleared by the parser again — only a direct button click (`COMPLETE_PROMPT`) would have cleared it, and that didn't happen. Fix: don't clobber `lastMenuRef` for unrecognized menus (only overwrite it when the new menu is itself a recognized `SETUP_PROMPT_TITLES` entry, or explicitly dismiss the old promptId before switching to track a new one). Destin also wants a "Send Anyway" override affordance on the blocking toast/gate as a user-facing safety valve regardless of root cause — a human who can see there's no live prompt should be able to force the send rather than being permanently locked out of chat.
  **CORRECTION 2026-07-17 — #157 fixed a real but MINOR path; the recurring bug Destin keeps hitting is a different, DETERMINISTIC cause #157 never touched.** Reproduced live on a resumed session. Root cause: `hasPendingInteraction` (`pty-input-gate.ts`) blocks on any `kind:'prompt'` timeline entry that isn't `completed`, and on resume `App.tsx` dispatches `HISTORY_LOADED` with `hasMore:true` for any session with history, which pushes a `_history_expand` "See previous messages" entry — no buttons, no `completed` — so `!undefined===true` trips the gate on EVERY resumed session until the user clicks "See previous messages." `ChatView` already special-cased that id when rendering; the gate never learned the exception. This is why #157 (an Ink-menu detector fix) couldn't help — `_history_expand` is a UI affordance from a different code path. Fix in **youcoded PR #165** (pending merge): exclude the marker id in the gate via a shared `HISTORY_EXPAND_PROMPT_ID` constant, + a second fix for a timing race where `usePromptDetector`'s show-timer fired `SHOW_PROMPT` for a menu that vanished during the 350ms debounce (re-check the screen before showing). Both pinned by tests. Systemic note: the two limbs of `hasPendingInteraction` clear asymmetrically — the awaiting-approval limb self-heals via `endTurn()` on every turn end, the prompt-entry limb is NEVER touched by `endTurn`, so a stranded prompt entry persists until its one dedicated clearer fires. **Lower-severity cousins found in the same investigation, NOT fixed by #165 (own follow-up entries needed):** (a) a remote client hydrating a `completed:false` prompt strands it (its detector refs are empty and `DISMISS_PROMPT` isn't relayed cross-device); (b) Android native `promptShow` with no matching dismiss/complete; (c) buddy `BubbleFeed` prompt-card click writes to the PTY but never dispatches `COMPLETE_PROMPT`, so a stuck prompt can't be cleared from the buddy view at all. (These three carry into the follow-up entry below.)
- [x] Spurious "session taken over on another device" on an idle, untouched session `bug` `#sync` `#leases` (added 2026-07-16; fixed 2026-07-16, youcoded PR #157 + wecoded-marketplace PR #43 deployed — client renewTick distinguishes holder-present (genuine takeover, now attributed with the device name) from holder-null (lapse → silent re-acquire); DO renew against a free lease revives it so old clients heal server-side)
- [x] Terminal view Ctrl+V pastes twice `bug` `#renderer` `#terminal` (added + fixed 2026-07-16, youcoded PR #157)
  Reported by Destin 2026-07-16. TerminalView's custom Ctrl+V handler called terminal.paste() and returned false, but returning false from attachCustomKeyEventHandler doesn't preventDefault — Chromium still fired its native paste event into xterm's hidden textarea, whose own paste listener wrote the clipboard to the PTY a second time. Fix: preventDefault the keydown so the custom path (which must exist for macOS, where Electron apps without an Edit menu role have no native paste shortcut) is the only path. Handler extracted to terminal-key-handler.ts with pinning tests. Confirm with one Ctrl+V in the next dev/build session.
- [x] StatusBar model chip always reads "Model Unknown" for native/OpenRouter sessions `bug` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` (added 2026-07-18, **SHIPPED 2026-07-19 — youcoded PR #185, merge `9e7de571`**)
  **Shipped as diagnosed, with one correction and one adjacent bug folded in.** Correction: the fix does NOT go through `useActiveSessionModel` as this entry proposed — that hook reads `turn.model` off timeline entries, so it's empty until the first assistant turn lands. `SessionInfo.model` is correct the moment the session exists, is kept live on every swap by `onNativeModelChanged` (`App.tsx:3039`), and was ALREADY reaching the renderer for the picker's current-selection highlight (`App.tsx:3036`) — so the fix needed zero new plumbing. **Adjacent bug found while tracing and fixed in the same PR (it was worse than the chip):** `cycleModel` (Shift+Space) had no provider gate. `SessionManager.sendInput` returns `false` for a worker-less native session, but `guardedPtySend` DISCARDS that return value, so the `if (!guardedPtySend(...)) return;` bail never fired and execution reached the optimistic writes — relabeling the chip with a CC alias the session is not running (`indexOf('unknown')` → -1 → wraps to `haiku`) AND writing that alias to the GLOBAL model preference. A confidently wrong chip outranks an honest error chip. Design calls worth not re-deriving: `ModelChip` is a display-only union rather than a widened `ModelAlias` (which also drives `cycleModel`, the `canAuto` gate at `App.tsx:2394`, and the persisted preference — a third-party model id must not leak into any of them); the label is a local pure prettifier, NOT the picker's catalog labels, because `ModelCatalog` is network-backed behind a TTL'd cache, async, empty without provider keys, and has NO rows for `openai-compatible` custom endpoints (`model-catalog.ts:187`) — the raw id always rides in the chip's `title`; chip color is `var(--tag-blue)` via TagChip's `color-mix` formula, the one tag slot colliding with nothing else in the bar (teal is Haiku, indigo is Opus, gray is Sonnet, fuchsia is Fable, red is both Unknown chips, accent/amber/salmon are permission modes). Pinned by `tests/native-model-label.test.ts` + `tests/model-chip.test.ts` (17 tests). **Not yet eyeballed in a dev instance** — the blue against each theme and truncation on a long GGUF name are queued visual checks.
- [x] `harness-stall-watchdog` flakes on Windows CI — a 15ms real-time stall budget `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-19)
  **SHIPPED 2026-07-22 — youcoded PR #198 (merge `79ac5802`), confirmed exactly as diagnosed.** Mechanism, now measured: Windows' default timer resolution is ~15.6ms, so `setTimeout(15)` fires on the first tick AT OR PAST the budget — the warning timer and the retry's first chunk landed in the same tick and scheduler noise picked the winner, which is why it was Windows-only and green on a plain re-run. Headroom was thin even on an idle 32-core Linux box: sweeping the budget, a spurious second warning first appears at 10ms (1/60) and is the majority outcome by 3ms (53/60), so 15ms was only ~4x the ideal path. Took the roadmap's second option (generous budget, not fake timers) because the file drives live stream reads on real timers by design: `STALL_MS = 250` (~16x the Windows tick) plus `STREAMING_WINDOW_MS = 400` for the keeps-emitting case, whose margin runs the OTHER way — its 4ms chunk spacing must stay far under the window or a hiccup fires a watchdog that case asserts never fires. Note the follow-on trap this created: at 250ms the both-attempts-stall case burns ~1.0s of real time against vitest's DEFAULT 5000ms `testTimeout`, dropping headroom from ~300x to ~5x — so the file now carries an explicit `vi.setConfig` too. Raising a budget can just move the bet up one level.
  `tests/harness-stall-watchdog.test.ts > "silent stall with nothing streamed: warns (willRetry) then AUTO-RETRIES and completes"` failed Windows-only on youcoded PR #185 with `AssertionError: expected [...] to have a length of 1 but got 2`, then passed on a plain re-run with no code change (and 10/10 in isolation on Linux). The test's `makeOpts` sets `stallWarningMs: 15, stallCountdownMs: 15` — a **15 millisecond** real-time budget. The case asserts exactly ONE stall warning: attempt 0 stalls, the retry succeeds. Under Windows parallel load the RETRY's own `completingStream` doesn't deliver its first chunk within 15ms either, so the watchdog fires a second warning and the length assertion fails. **Same fixed-budget family as the `subagent-watcher` and git-heavy-sync entries above, and the same fix shape** — the budget is the bug, not the symptom: either drive this file on fake timers (it asserts on ordering/count, not on wall-clock, so it has no reason to race a real clock at all) or give the real-time budgets enough headroom that runner load can't close the gap. Note the file was NOT touched by #185 (renderer-only diff) and tests `src/main/harness/harness-session.ts`. Costs a CI re-run each time it fires, and trains people to re-run red Windows builds without reading them. (found 2026-07-19 merging youcoded#185)
- [x] Device `lastSeen` is a launch stamp, not a heartbeat `bug` `#sync` (added 2026-07-16; FIXED 2026-07-18)
  `upsertSelf` runs once at `main.ts:1573`; nothing bumps `lastSeen` while the app runs, so "last seen 4 hours ago" actually means "last launched 4 hours ago". **Fixed via per-device sync recency carried over the SyncHub DO — deliberately NOT the git heartbeat this note worried about (no file-write-per-bump, no history bloat).** The `SyncGroupRoom` DO now stores a durable per-account `lastSyncByDevice` map (keyed by machineId), fed by the existing `pushed:true` signal (which now also carries `deviceId` + server `at`) and shipped in `hello`; device rows show "Synced just now" (<5min) / "Last synced X ago" via the pure `deviceActivityLabel`; self reads the local live `lastSyncEpoch` (no longer hard-codes "active now"). Backward-compatible rollout. Ships: wecoded-marketplace PR #45, youcoded PR #171 + #170. Spec+plan: `docs/archive/{specs,plans}/2026-07-17-sync-menu-recency-*.md`.
- [x] Workspace CI is red on 8 store-budget overruns `bug` `#docs` `#tooling` (added 2026-07-30, **FIXED 2026-08-12 — same work as the fuller "youcoded-dev's OWN CI is red" entry above (youcoded PR #302 + workspace rule trims): audit-anchors now exits 0.**)
  `.github/workflows/workspace-ci.yml` (added 2026-07-30, merge `b447c8e`) runs `audit-anchors.mjs` on every push and on a daily cron; it exits 1 today, with two distinct failure classes. ~~**Self-clearing:** 2 stale `verify:` anchors in `.claude/rules/react-renderer.md` plus 4 missing `docs/MAP.md` paths, all pointing at `desktop/src/renderer/dev/workbench/`.~~ **CLEARED 2026-07-30** when `feat/ui-workbench` merged (youcoded PR #273 `9fa50b14`): `anchors 180/180 ok · MAP paths 117/117 ok`. Only the store-budget class below remains. **Needs work (re-measured 2026-08-12 — four grew):** 8 store-budget violations — `.claude/rules/` bodies over the 600-word limit (`artifacts` 897, `chat-reducer` 789, `conversations` 848, `native-runtime` 3300, `react-renderer` 967, `registries` 605, `sync-spaces` 1213) and `docs/PITFALLS.md` at 2694/2500. **And the anchor class is red AGAIN (2026-08-12): `.claude/rules/sync-spaces.md` points at `youcoded/desktop/tests/sync-spaces-project-registry.test.ts`, which no longer exists** — see the fuller "youcoded-dev's OWN CI is red" entry under Bugs; these two entries are the same failure and should be worked together. Fix per `.claude/rules/README.md`: overflow migrates to the rule's lazy doc or becomes a pinning test — editorial trimming, not deletion. Context for whoever picks this up: the 2026-07-15 baseline report recorded this same command exiting **0** with `residue: 0`, so all 12 findings accrued in 15 days with nothing watching — which is precisely why the CI now exists. Landing it red was a deliberate call (Destin, 2026-07-30) so the failure is a forcing function, but a permanently-red check stops being read, so this should not sit indefinitely.
- [x] **Bash: background execution instead of a 10-minute SIGKILL** `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-28 — ledger G-1; shipped 2026-08-28)
  Every mainstream harness except Pi lets a command outlive the call; here `npm run dev`, a long Android build, or an installer that asks a question dies at the timeout. Smallest version: at timeout, detach instead of kill and deliver completion the way background specialists already report back (no new tool). Larger: a `process` tool (poll / read log / type into / stop). Needs brainstorming + a spec first — the Bash card needs a "still running" state (like the Task card fix in `62c1f182`) and a visible Stop, or closing the app orphans dev servers.
  **Designed and approved 2026-08-28.** Decisions: Claude Code's shape — `run_in_background` on Bash plus `BashOutput`/`KillShell`, AND a foreground command that hits its time limit is moved to the background instead of SIGKILLed (the 10-min cap goes away); survives a conversation switch, killed on conversation close and app quit with the reason on the card; Stop kills the whole process family (also fixes today's Escape-key kill orphaning grandchildren); finished notice delivered at the next idle boundary via the specialists' `queueHostNotice` path; polls exempt from the doom-loop window with a 3-strike pushback instead; cap 5 per conversation. Card approved 5/5 on deck `docs/active/design/2026-08-28-bash-background/`; mockup on youcoded branch `feat/bash-background-ui` (worktree `worktrees/bash-bg`, workbench-only, `MOCK_ONLY: native.killShell`). **Shipped** — spec `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-28-bash-background-execution-design.md`, plan `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-28-bash-background-execution.md`, depth `youcoded/docs/native-runtime.md` → "Background Bash". Also fixed in passing: the polls-vs-doom-loop guard is a flat 8-reads/turn cap (D7), not the 3-strike counter named above. Typing into a running command is the recorded follow-up.
- [x] **Marketplace overhaul — catalog in the database, first-class item types, visible trust, feedback people actually leave** `feature` `#marketplace` `#wecoded` `#worker` (added 2026-08-27, **SHIPPED 2026-08-31**)
  **SHIPPED 2026-08-31 — wecoded-marketplace#74 (`0682c84`) and youcoded#366 (`76fcae26`).** The catalog is live and serving **4,156 listings**, every one carrying its `catalog` block: 2,539 anthropic · 977 awesome-copilot · 314 docker · 252 cursorrules · 74 youcoded, of which **2,311 are member rows** (skills/specialists/connections inside a bundle). Scan verdicts are a genuine mix — 2,697 checked, 1,420 unchecked, 39 caution — never all-checked, which is the property that mattered. Measured on the live endpoint: 4.6 MB raw, **533 KB on the wire** (Cloudflare Brotli, 8.6×), and a repeat fetch with the ETag answers **304 with 0 bytes**, so the hourly refresh on every device costs nothing when nothing changed. Two-segment member ids resolve; CORS exposes `ETag` so browser clients can use it.
  **Three things were wrong in the plan and were found by running it, not by reading it:** (1) repo lookups used `repoUrl ?? sourceRef`, but 142 of 289 live non-YouCoded entries point `repoUrl` at a *marketing site* and keep the repo in `sourceRef` — those 142 would have got no HEAD and fallen back to the frozen `sourceSha`, i.e. exactly the stale pin the design exists to prevent; (2) a repo name containing a dot was cut at the dot, 404'd, and an empty file list scanned clean — a plugin marked "checked" with **zero files read**, violating the plan's own rule; (3) the KV publish trigger keyed off the version moving *during `finish`*, by which time it has already stopped moving, so it could never fire.
  **Two fixes are not in the plan at all and both would have shipped broken.** (a) **The Update button worked for 21% of the store.** Task 1 made the badge clickable, but `update()` re-runs the installer, which returns `already_installed` before it reaches any clone; only `local` sources had a real upgrade path. That left **237 of 302 live entries** (152 `url` + 85 `git-subdir`) pressing a working button that rewrote nothing. `upgradePluginFromGit` clones into staging and swaps only after the clone *and* the pin succeed, so a failed update leaves the install untouched. (b) **A re-scan that found nothing new was moving the ETag** — our own `local` plugins have no upstream commit to skip on, so a fresh `scan.checkedAt` made 71 rows differ hourly, bumping the version and invalidating **every client's cache every hour**, defeating the entire 304 design including Android on mobile data.
  **Two follow-up fixes, 2026-08-31, both found by measuring the live catalog rather than reading code.** (1) **A star count was invalidating every client's cache** — wecoded-marketplace#75. Snapshotting `GET /catalog` either side of an ingest run and diffing field by field showed 97 rows differing with `catalog.stars` as the ONLY field that moved (e.g. 35664 → 35665). Each write bumps the version, which IS the ETag, so a returning client got a 4.6 MB `200` instead of a free `304` — roughly 24 full re-downloads per device per day, Android over mobile data, for a field **never rendered on either platform**. The merge now treats a stars-only difference as unchanged while still taking the fresh count whenever the row genuinely changes. Verified after deploy by repeating the same experiment: **97 differing rows → 1**, and that one is not stars. (2) **The trust chip named a check that never existed** — youcoded#367. It said "Verified" and claimed the publisher had "proved they own this name"; no identity, domain or ownership check exists anywhere in the ingest or Worker. It sat on 89% of listings, contradicted the badge beside it on 1,268 rows ("Verified" + "Not checked"), and drew a shield-with-tick nearly identical to the real safety shield. It now names the actual upstream list (Anthropic / GitHub / Docker / awesome-cursorrules / YouCoded) and is off cards entirely; five tests pin that the hover text can never again say "proved", "own this name" or "verif". Display-only — nothing filters, sorts or ranks on the tier.
  Verified before merge: desktop full suite green; **Android 579 tests + `assembleDebug` + `assembleReleaseTest` compiled and run locally** (the plan's "no Android SDK on this machine" was wrong — there is one at `~/.android-sdk`; the check tested for `ANDROID_HOME`/`local.properties`); worker 267 tests; ingest 22. Follow-ups filed separately in Bugs above. Still unverified by anyone's eyes: the two blank theme previews, and the "Not checked" tooltip wording.
  Destin's decision 2026-08-27: build Layers A–D (structured catalog + hourly pinned sync from the CC0 official MCP Registry, awesome-copilot, Docker catalog, awesome-cursorrules; origin badges; a computed "What this can do" panel before install; scan-every-version; one-tap 👍/👎 + comments; category filters + collections) **inside the app first**. Design so the public-registry item below is a switch, not a rewrite (catalog rows carry source repo + exact commit + SPDX license from day one). Full assessment: `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-27-marketplace-strategy.md`.
  **UI approved 2026-08-28** over three decks (20/20 steps) — spec `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-28-marketplace-overhaul-ui-design.md`; mockup was on youcoded branch `feat/marketplace-overhaul-ui` (workbench-only, invented data) — **that branch and its worktree were merged and deleted 2026-08-31; the code is on master**. Plans (2026-08-28): `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-28-marketplace-feedback-worker.md` (thumbs + comments routes, independent — **shipped**), `…-marketplace-catalog-service.md` (catalog in D1, hourly ingest from four sources, rule-based scan), `…-marketplace-app-wiring.md` (desktop + Android read `/catalog`, pinned installs, merge). **Consolidated 2026-08-30:** the last two were merged into one self-contained plan, `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-30-marketplace-overhaul-remaining-work.md` (22 tasks in 5 phases) — the other two are marked `superseded` and must not be implemented from. Deferred from the rounds (spec §5): per-item install of a bundle member · tap-to-explain badges on touch · edge fade on the phone type row · Android Library→Marketplace handover lands on Plugins · ranking + duplicate bundle/member results · comment moderation report backend · delete-your-own-comment · orphaned star-review rows.
  **Plan 1 (feedback routes) SHIPPED 2026-08-28** — wecoded-marketplace PR #71, merge `5116924`, deployed green (migration 0005 applied, routes live and hand-verified against production). Ships `POST /thumbs` (install-gated, returns the plugin's new totals with the write so a vote moves the number without re-fetching the 5-minute-cached `/stats`), `GET /thumbs/:id`, `POST /comments`, public `GET /comments/:id` (one **or two** segments, so a bundle member's id reaches its own thread), `GET /admin/comments` + `DELETE /admin/comments/:id`, plus `thumbs_up`/`thumbs_down` on `/stats` and both new tables in `/auth/export`. Two corrections to the deferred list above: v1 ships no Report control on comments because the *reporting flow* (UI, queue, resolution) is out of scope — **not** because `reports` cannot hold a comment id; its PK is a random id and the rating columns carry no foreign key. And the mockup's Report button was worse than dead — it filed against the commenter's star rating — so it was removed, making a future Report a re-add. Admin takedown is **not** deferred: it shipped, because a public comment box whose only remedy was hand-editing production D1 was not shippable. App side (channels + FeedbackSection) **shipped 2026-08-31 with the rest of the overhaul** (youcoded#366).
  Two facts uncovered while building, both filed separately above: `checkRateLimit` has never limited anything in production (measured), and theme installs are never reported to the Worker (so the planned `themes[].installs` field was cut rather than shipped reading 0 forever).
  **Plan review 2026-08-28** — `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-28-marketplace-overhaul-plan-review.md`; all findings applied to the four docs the same day. The two that changed the shape of the build: (1) the ingest must resolve each repo's **current HEAD** and installs must pin to that alone — never to the `sourceSha` already in `index.json`, which 236 of 302 live entries carry and which would have frozen those plugins forever and made Update a no-op that claims success; (2) the Worker's upsert **merges instead of clobbering** — a rate-limited or half-finished ingest run can no longer downgrade a stored scan verdict, licence or star count, which is what would otherwise have made badges flap and listings vanish. Also added: `ETag`/304 on `/catalog` (the response is ~5,000 rows / several MB, refreshed hourly on both platforms, and `*.workers.dev` is not edge-cached), a `CATALOG_ENABLED` kill switch, a "only re-read files whose commit moved" skip (~6,000 hourly GitHub fetches → dozens, against a 1,000/hr Actions budget), and two-segment routes for bundle-member ids on `/catalog` and `/comments`.
  **Folded in 2026-08-28 after a second review pass**, because each one lives in a file this work already rewrites: the Update button (its own `bug` entry above) · installed prompts lying about updates (ditto) · theme cards showing a download count (`/stats` has no theme installs field at all today, so plugins show downloads and themes show likes in one grid) · the two blank theme previews (ROADMAP:1042) · phone-width rail clipping (UI audit P-17) and raw markdown in `longDescription` · the marketplace repo's cruft — the abandoned 2-of-7 `themes/index.json`, 13 `overrides/` targeting deprecated ids plus one targeting an id that does not exist, and `curated-defaults.json` · making CI **block** on secret content instead of warning (the catalog scanner already treats the same patterns as a Caution finding, so the rule would otherwise exist twice at two strengths). Feedback hardening also folded in: the caller's own vote is now readable (`GET /thumbs/:id`) so the buttons stop forgetting it, votes are one-at-a-time and report real failures instead of swallowing them, a percentage is withheld below 5 votes, and the "install it first" reason is visible text rather than a hover tooltip that does nothing on a phone. The catalog plan also gains an id-existence check so votes, comments and installs cannot be recorded against listings that do not exist (now Task 8).
  **Durability pass 2026-08-28 (third review round), all folded into the plans.** Six guards, each answering "what breaks in month three rather than week one". (1) `GET /catalog` computed its ETag by reading every row *before* deciding it could answer "nothing changed" — so the cheap reply cost the same D1 quota as the whole several-MB payload, capping the marketplace at a few hundred client refreshes a day for the entire user base. A one-row `catalog_meta` version table now answers the 304 in a single read, and keyset paging replaces `OFFSET`, which was re-scanning everything it skipped. (2) A **retire guard**: a `finish` that would delist >20% of a source refuses, retires nothing, and fails the run — an upstream folder rename would otherwise have silently delisted 245 of 257 prompts for an hour. (3) The scan-skip key is now `<commit>:<SCAN_RULES_VERSION>`, so improving the scanner re-scans the catalog by itself instead of waiting for a `--force-rescan` nobody remembers. (4) The ingest **exits non-zero** on any errored, refused or zero-row source, so a broken scraper turns the hourly run red instead of leaving a green run and a frozen catalog; `GET /admin/catalog/health` is the human's version of the same question. (5) Comments get a takedown path in v1 (`GET /admin/comments`, `DELETE /admin/comments/:id`, hide-not-delete) — shipping a public comment box whose only remedy was hand-editing production was not acceptable. (6) An installed item that has been delisted upstream says so in Library, guarded to fire only when the catalog itself actually answered.
  **One decision still open, and it has a deadline.** The grey "Not checked" shield (spec §1.6) was approved against invented fixture data with a healthy mix of verdicts; the real catalog will not have that mix, because only our own rows and the cursorrules prompts are scanned — everything mirrored arrives unchecked. A grid of grey shields may read as "this marketplace is unsafe" rather than "we have not looked yet", and the alternative is to render no shield at all when unchecked so its absence is the neutral state. It needs one small deck against real ingest data, which only exists after the catalog service's first run — so it is pinned as **Task 15 (Phase 3)** of the consolidated plan, the last gate before the UI merges. Left alone it resolves silently as "keep the shield", which is the option nobody has seen with real numbers.
  **Cut from the first build:** the official MCP Registry source (25,291 servers) — its rows are uninstallable, unrateable and unscanned, and the star lookup that decides which to show would take ~62 weeks to cover the corpus. It moves to the Layer E entry below, where the full corpus is the point. Docker's ~320 servers fill the Connections tab with better data.

  Verified 2026-08-28 on master: `MarketplaceCard.tsx:351-360` renders the word "Update" in a plain `<span>` with no click handler; `LibraryScreen.tsx:265-280`'s Updates tab lists the items and offers no action; `MarketplaceDetailOverlay` shows Install/Uninstall and no Update. `update(id, type)` (`marketplace-context.tsx:308-326`) is exported from the provider and has **zero call sites in the whole renderer**. The main-process side is complete and reachable — `skills:update` (`ipc-handlers.ts:1497`), `skillProvider.update` (`skill-provider.ts:326`), `updateTheme` (`theme-marketplace-provider.ts:255-266`). So no plugin and no theme can be updated by a user; only bundled plugins self-upgrade, silently, at launch. **This supersedes the themes-only framing of the entry at ROADMAP:725** — that entry's diagnosis was right about themes and the bug is app-wide. Folded into the overhaul because the app-wiring phase rewrites that exact card corner and because commit-pinning is worth little if the user cannot act on being out of date.
- [x] **Marketplace: a plugin installed mid-session isn't votable until the next launch** `bug` `#marketplace` (added 2026-08-28) **DONE 2026-09-01** — already fixed on master before 2026-09-01 — `marketplace:install` calls `reconcileInstalls()` after `postInstall`, pinned by `marketplace-install-reconcile.test.ts`.
  `reconcileInstalls()` (`desktop/src/main/install-reconcile.ts`) runs on sign-in and at launch. A plugin installed *while the app is open* does report itself individually (`marketplace-context.tsx` → `marketplaceApi.install(id)`), but only the **plugin** id — not the skill-level ids the provider then surfaces as their own marketplace items (`superpowers:brainstorming`). So voting on a freshly-installed plugin's *skill* page is refused until the next restart re-runs the reconcile. Fix is small: call the reconcile after a successful install, or have the single-install path report the skill ids too. Same id-space mismatch as the bug it follows.
- [x] **Marketplace: theme installs are never reported to the Worker** `bug` `#marketplace` `#worker` (added 2026-08-28) **DONE 2026-09-01** — step 1 already fixed on master — `installTheme()` calls `marketplaceApi.install('theme:<slug>')`, pinned by `marketplace-context-install-telemetry.test.tsx`. Steps 2–3 (Worker-side theme stats) were never separate items; file them if wanted.
  `installTheme()` (`desktop/src/renderer/state/marketplace-context.tsx:285`) installs a theme to disk via `claude().theme.marketplace.install(slug)` and never calls `marketplaceApi.install()` — the sole caller of that is the *skill* path at line 253 (`rg "postInstall|/installs" desktop/src app/src/main/kotlin`). So the `installs` table holds **zero** theme rows and theme cards can never show a download count, while plugin cards beside them do. The `theme:${slug}` string in that file is a local key for the install spinner, not an id sent anywhere. Three ordered steps, tracked in `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-30-marketplace-overhaul-remaining-work.md` (Task 3): (1) record the install, (2) `/stats` gains `themes[slug].installs`, (3) the card reads it. Steps 2 and 3 are worthless without step 1 — which is why the Worker field was deliberately cut from the feedback plan rather than shipped reading 0 forever.
- [x] **Marketplace: comment threads truncate silently at 50** `bug` `#marketplace` `#worker` (added 2026-08-28) **DONE 2026-09-01** — wecoded-marketplace PR #79 (`total` beside `comments`) + youcoded PR #375 ("Showing the 50 most recent of N").
  `GET /comments/:plugin_id` is `LIMIT 50` with no pagination and no total, so a busy thread just stops with nothing saying there is more. Cheapest first step: return a `total` alongside `comments` and render "showing the 50 most recent of N".
- [x] **Marketplace: a held comment is invisible to its author after the toast** `feature` `#marketplace` (added 2026-08-28) **DONE 2026-09-01** — youcoded PR #375 — a device-local record shows the held post first, dimmed, "Held for review · only you can see it". Trade-off: only on the device that posted it; no Worker unhide route exists yet.
  A comment the llama-guard classifier flags is stored `hidden = 1` and never listed, so the author sees "Posted. It's held for review." once and then nothing, forever. `GET /auth/export` now carries `hidden` so the data is recoverable, but the plugin page should say it too.

Spec rewritten 2026-08-26 away from its first draft: the three-"kinds"-of-session taxonomy and its per-widget relevance table are GONE, replaced by two mechanical rules — a chip with no value hides instead of printing `--`, and what belongs to the other runtime does not render. Newly *working* in native sessions rather than hidden: Session Cost (priced per turn at the model that ran it — `CatalogModel.pricing` keeps only `{in,out}` today and that is the whole gap) and Code Changes (derived from `structuredPatch`, and it must walk `subagentSegments` or it undercounts hardest on the biggest sessions). Accepted cost, stated not mitigated: switching to a local model *because* you are near your 5-hour limit removes the number you were watching. It also closed a hole where `/usage` typed the literal text "/usage" at the model for anyone who had never run Claude Code. The session recommended "don't merge until we know" and offered two ways to settle it (compare memory growth against a `master` dev instance over a comparable stretch, or find what retains memory); **Destin had not chosen when the session ended, and the build ledger does not record the OOM at all** — reading the ledger alone says the branch is ready. - [x] Show progress while a tool call's arguments stream (the "silent Write" blind spot) `feature` `#native-runtime` `#renderer` `#ux` (added 2026-08-12, Destin; **SHIPPED 2026-08-13 — youcoded `0ccb576c`**)
  When a native model generates a large tool call — a Write whose input is a whole file — the app shows only the pulsing thinking indicator until the arguments finish. Reproduced live 2026-08-12 (dev session `c08e0b47`, DeepSeek v4-flash flappy-bird ask): ~4 min of reasoning, one line of visible text, then multi-minute silence while the Write args streamed; Destin read it as a hang, and reports this shape recurring. The stall watchdog is rightly quiet (data IS flowing) — the gap is presentational.
  **Verified feasible:** ai@7 emits `tool-input-start` / `tool-input-delta` / `tool-input-end` stream parts (present in `node_modules/ai/dist/index.d.ts`); the consume loop's switch (`harness-session.ts` ~1789) currently handles only the completed `tool-call` part, so the deltas are dropped on the floor today.
  **Shape to brainstorm, not decided:** the emit surface is FROZEN (`native-runtime.md`), so progress should ride the existing `assistant-thinking` heartbeat the way `promptProcessing` already does (display-only, never persisted) — e.g. `{ toolArgProgress: { toolName, chars } }` → ThinkingIndicator renders "Writing a file — 12 KB so far". Open questions: per-tool naming (Write has a file_path only after enough JSON has streamed to parse it), throttling (per-chunk emit would spam the IPC), and whether Bash/Edit want it too or only file-writing tools.
  **Update 2026-08-12 (same day):** Destin widened the goal — minimize generic-spinner time overall, preferring visible tool liveness or streaming text; an EARLY TOOL CARD (rendered at `tool-input-start`, detail filled as args stream) is now on the table alongside the progress line. Full investigation handoff with everything verified this session (adapter emission chain, watchdog feeding, OpenRouter probe result): `docs/archive/handoffs/2026-08-12-tool-streaming-visibility.md`. Probe preserved at `scripts/tool-arg-stream-probe.mjs`.
  **SPECCED 2026-08-12:** `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-12-tool-arg-streaming-visibility.md` — the early tool card won. A normal `ToolCard` appears at `tool-input-start` in its running state, detail line `preparing… N chars`, expanded body "Still preparing tool call…", and it transitions IN PLACE to the real card because `tool-input-start` and the completed `tool-call` carry the identical id (verified in the installed `provider-utils` dist) and `TRANSCRIPT_TOOL_USE` is already idempotent by `toolUseId`. Progress rides `assistant-thinking` as `toolPreparing` (dropped by `session-store.ts:93`, so nothing partial is persisted); `preparing` is a boolean on a `running` entry, never a fifth `ToolCallStatus`; `endTurn` DELETES preparing cards rather than failing them. No partial-JSON parsing of `file_path` — dropped as a truncation-failure class for information the finished card shows moments later. Native-only; CC and Android unchanged.
  **SHIPPED 2026-08-13** (youcoded merge `0ccb576c`, plan `docs/archive/plans/2026-08-12-preparing-tool-cards.md`). Built as specced. Two things the plan did not foresee, both now in the code: (1) teaching `endTurn` to prune an emptied preparing group means it returns `assistantTurns`, which silently clobbered the two callers that build that map themselves — `TRANSCRIPT_TURN_COMPLETE` (usage/model stamping) and `TRANSCRIPT_INTERRUPT` (`stopReason`, whose "Interrupted" footer went blank) — so `endTurn` now takes their map as a third argument instead of spreading over it; (2) `injectPlanSegment` needs `beforeGroupId` now that placement runs first, or an ExitPlanMode plan renders *below* its own tool-group. Group placement for both the preparing and real paths is one shared helper (`placeToolInCurrentGroup`) precisely so they cannot drift apart — if they place differently, the real tool-use adds a second card instead of superseding. Verified live by Destin on a native session. Carried open item, not a blocker: whether llama.cpp's `--jinja` grammar-constrained path emits a first tool-call delta carrying the function name — a provider that never emits `tool-input-start` simply falls through to the old behavior.

Three candidate designs for one surface rendered simultaneously against the same mock backend, with a pick recorded per round so a design narrows over passes: pick one of three → Claude authors three more from it → pick again. Replaces the query-param switcher loop (`utils/design-variant.ts`, added and deleted three times), which meant flipping between candidates one at a time from memory AND editing the production component to hold them all. Candidates live in `dev/workbench/compare/registry.tsx` and are built from the REAL primitives and tokens — the only new code in a candidate is the arrangement under comparison, which is what makes a winner paste back into production unchanged. Two constraints found building it: a real `Dialog` can't be a candidate (fixed-position, all three would stack centre-screen — hence the `panel` frame), and picks live in localStorage because HMR reloads constantly.

  **Status 2026-07-28 (final) — THE KIT IS COMPLETE. All 12 roles + D1 shipped to `feat/menu-internals-tranche-1` (24 commits, UNMERGED, no PR). Destin reviewed and approved in a dev instance.** K5 and K9 were expected to need a fourth tranche behind a copy pass; the copy pass ran the same day and **turned out to be structural, not editorial** — seven of Remote Access's eleven setup branches carried over verbatim and all three danger zones already had a consequence sentence satisfying `docs/error-message-standards.md`. Five copy decisions (worksheet: `docs/archive/specs/2026-07-28-k5-k9-copy-pass.md`) and they landed in the same tranche. **One real bug fixed on the way:** `{setupError || 'Setup failed'}` replaced a missing reason with a hardcoded guess, leaving the user two words and no next step — now the real error + Retry when there is one, and the two-action fallback when there is not. Five converted; `SettingsPanel` and `SyncPanel` own their Dialogs so their views just pass `title`/`headerActions` up, and `ThemeScreen`'s `editingSlug` was lifted to its host the way K12 lifted `showInfo`. `SubViewHeader` deleted, three back-chevron reimplementations gone. **The setup wizard keeps its header by design** — its title derives from state that belongs inside it, and the real fix (the wizard owning its own `<Dialog>`) turns it from replacing the sync panel into stacking over it, which is a UX call.
