- [x] **Native tools: the 2026-08-26 harness-comparison ledger, batches A/B/C + PDF reading** `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` (added 2026-08-28, **SHIPPED 2026-08-28 — youcoded PRs #352 `8bfbd73a`, #353 `0c3c1fc7`, #354 `51d18c54`**)
  Ledger: `docs/archive/investigations/2026-08-26-native-tools-vs-other-harnesses.md` §8. Shipped: **D-1** Bash no longer tells the model to re-run "piped through head/tail" (no `pipefail` — that hid failing builds); **D-2** every native tool schema is `.strict()` — an unknown parameter is an error naming the valid ones, never silently dropped (plugin/MCP tools deliberately left pass-through); **D-3** Read on a folder says so and lists the first 50 entries instead of a raw `EISDIR`; **D-4** the "not Read this session" refusal names session resume as a cause; **D-5** Write preserves CRLF/BOM like Edit; **D-6** WebFetch's decorative `prompt` param dropped; **G-4** Edit parameters described (strip the line-number prefix, keep `old_string` to 1–3 lines); **G-5** Read caps at 2000 lines or ~50 KB with an exact `offset=N` continuation; **G-6** Read extracts PDF text page by page (`pages="1-5"`, max 20/call) with scanned pages named honestly — uses the `pdfjs-dist` the file viewer already ships, lazily loaded, one extraction at a time; **G-7** Grep gains `ignore_case`/`literal`/`type`/`multiline`; **G-10** Write refuses `// ... rest of code ...` placeholder lines; **G-11** re-reading an unchanged slice returns a one-line notice (cleared on resume, `/clear`, and every compaction); **G-12** Skill accepts `args`; **G-13** Bash description prefers Read/Grep/Glob/Edit over cat/grep/find/sed (unconditional — the description builder does not know the permission mode, see the open T-2 item); **T-3** Read carries the "read only the part you need" nudge.
  **What users will notice:** fewer dead-end turns from local models (folders, placeholder overwrites, CRLF flips, silent case-sensitive searches), and the assistant can read a PDF. **One new visible refusal:** a Write whose content has a comment-only line like `# ... remaining ...` is refused with the line quoted. **Expected transient:** Claude-Code-trained models may hit "Invalid arguments" once before adapting — measure with the harness evaluator if it seems frequent.
- [x] Zoom + hover magnifier for pictures and PDFs in the artifact viewer `feature` `#renderer` `#ui` `#artifacts` (added 2026-08-27, **SHIPPED 2026-08-28 — youcoded PR #348 merge `5f8e68bd`**)
  Destin's ask: a hover magnifier for images "and similar file types", plus a `+`/`-` zoom pill. Both viewers get the same pill (`components/ui/ZoomPill.tsx`) and the same lens; images add drag-to-pan, Ctrl+wheel and two-finger pinch, PDFs feed the scale into pdf.js so pages get bigger AND sharper. Ladder is `fit, 12.5, 25, 50, 75, 100, 150, 200, 400, 800` — the low rungs exist because a large picture fits at 10-20%% and a ladder starting at 50%% made ONE press four times bigger (Destin: "weird and inconsistent"); he chose familiar round numbers over a constant ratio, so the steps stay mathematically uneven on purpose.
  **The pill is always visible, never hover-revealed.** A fade never appears on Android (no pointer-enter) and an `opacity-0` control stays in the tab order — the bug the Edit cluster already has. The magnifier button is gated on `(any-hover: hover)`, NOT `(hover: hover)`: the un-prefixed form asks about the PRIMARY pointer, which on a touchscreen laptop can be the finger, and would hide the lens on a machine with a trackpad right there.
  **Three pre-existing bugs fixed in passing.** (1) `useZoomControls`' pinch handler is capture-phase on `window` and does not `stopPropagation`, so a viewer-local handler double-fired — one pinch resized the app *and* the picture; it now yields inside `[data-zoomable]`. (2) `PdfPages` could never re-render a page: it had a `cancelled` flag gating the loop but called `RenderTask.cancel()` nowhere, and a second render into a live canvas is the pdf.js "same canvas during multiple render operations" error — hence a restructure into React-owned per-page components with a retained document proxy, not an extension. (3) Scale was clamped to fit only when SET, so widening the pane could raise fit above the stored scale and leave a picture smaller than fitted with zoom-out still offered.
  **The PDF size ceiling (16MP / 16384px per side) is pre-emptive because it has to be:** an oversized canvas is accepted by Chrome, reports its requested width, paints nothing, and throws nothing, so "render and catch" does not exist. Above it `+` disables *with its reason*.
  **Live-review bugs, all found by Destin in a dev instance and each now pinned by a test:** the lens tracked the whole pane instead of the picture and parked on its own off switch (`resolveSource` ignored the cursor; controls now mark themselves `data-loupe-block`); above fit a press anywhere started a pan and captured the pointer, so pressing `+` ate its own click and every control died after one zoom; and the lens kept painting behind a dialog — fixed by testing whether a scrim is SHOWING, after a first attempt that tested for its mere presence disabled the magnifier entirely (a scrim element is mounted at all times and fades in).
  **Not verified on a device:** Android touch pinch on a photo is a NEW capability there (`WebViewHost.kt` disables WebView zoom and both `index.html` copies ship `user-scalable=no`, so `ImageView`'s old `touchAction: 'pinch-zoom'` was dead code). Decks: `docs/archive/design/2026-08-27-artifact-zoom/`.
- [x] A silent provider parks the turn instead of killing it `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness` `#chat-ux` `#attention` `#error-handling` (added 2026-08-16, **SHIPPED 2026-08-16 — youcoded merge `28d3f82e`**)
  When a provider went quiet mid-stream the turn died at the 75s mark and every byte of streamed work went with it. Now it **parks**: the stream reader is never torn down, a red *"Provider may have stalled"* card counts up with **Retry** and **Stop**, and the turn ends only when the model speaks again, the user retries, or the user stops. A chunk arriving minutes later still lands in the loop and continues the turn — the park works by *not* resolving the stall race, so there is nothing to rebuild.
  **The clock split is the whole design, and it is deliberately asymmetric.** Clock 1 (nothing ever streamed this attempt) is untouched and still ends the turn: a model that never sent a first byte has not "stalled", it never began, so it keeps its one silent auto-retry and then its `didn't begin responding` error. Clock 2 (streamed, then silent) parks. `turnEverParked` carries the carve-out across a turn's later steps so a retried step can never die on Clock 1. **Timing did not move** — `STALL_WARNING_MS`/`STALL_RETRY_COUNTDOWN_MS` are byte-identical to master and `prefill-watchdog.test.ts` is unchanged; the card appears exactly where death used to.
  **Retry erases the abandoned half-sentence from all three places it lives**, which is the part that took the most review: the screen (`NATIVE_PARTS_DROPPED`), the durable JSONL (the open part is discarded, never flushed), and the model's own memory (`reportPartial('')`). The erase is **trailing-run scoped** — part ids repeat across steps (the SDK falls back to a literal `text-0`), so a naive whole-turn filter deleted *finished* paragraphs from earlier steps. Found in cross-task review; deleted work is strictly worse than the duplicate text it was preventing.
  **Specialist children deliberately do NOT park.** `wireChildLive` re-emits only `SUBAGENT_DISPLAY_TYPES`, which excludes `assistant-thinking` — so a parked child would show no card, no Retry, no Stop, its `send()` would never settle, and the parent's `Task` tool would wait forever with nothing on screen. Nothing else caps a specialist run. They fall through to the ordinary stall path and get reported as a failed run, exactly as before this branch.
  **Two declared behavior changes** beyond the feature, both kept on purpose and both traceable here rather than left as surprises: (1) the composer's square **Stop button now survives the stall warning**, which necessarily reaches Claude Code sessions too — `stuck` has two indistinguishable producers, and a stuck CC session was equally un-stoppable from a phone, which is the same defect. Documented as an exception to spec §12 and pinned in both directions. (2) The buddy-list pill now defers to `attentionDotColor()` instead of its own drifted table: stalled blue→red, session-died grey→red, error blue→red, **stuck red→amber** (the pill was painting red for the one state that means *I don't know*, against the app's own rule).
  **Late Critical, caught by the whole-branch review and worth recording:** `useAttentionClassifier` — the Claude Code PTY watcher — cleaned up by resetting *any* non-`ok` state at mount, including states it never set. For a native session that fired exactly once, at ChatView mount. Desktop was unaffected (`chat:hydrate` is remote-only, per `preload.ts:513`), but a **phone reconnecting to an already-parked desktop session** had the correct card thrown away and saw a bare spinner. Fixed by gating both dispatch sites on `hasBuffer`; proved with a CDP probe against the live workbench three ways, the decisive one being *fix reverted → card gone*.
  Guards: `harness-stall-watchdog.test.ts` (the park, the clock split as its own pinning test, the specialist-child carve-out, the model-memory erase), `session-store.ts` tests (a dropped part is discarded, never flushed), `attention-reducer.test.ts` (park→resume→park restamps the clock), `useAttentionClassifier.test.tsx`, `useStreamingGate.test.tsx` + `InputBar.test.tsx` (both halves of the Stop gate), `attention-strip.test.tsx`, `ipc-channels.test.ts` (`native:retry` five-surface parity). Spec + plan archived under `docs/archive/`.
- [x] Dismissing an `AskUserQuestion` ends the native turn `feature` `#native-runtime` `#harness-tools` `#permissions` `#chat-ux` (added 2026-08-13, **SHIPPED 2026-08-15 — youcoded merge `a2b23d1f`**)
  Destin's ask: in native sessions the one gesture meaning *stop, I'll tell you what I want* was the gesture that made the assistant guess and press on — Dismiss returned "Continue with your best judgment" to the model and the loop ran another step. Now a dismissal returns a paired tool result, back-fills any un-executed sibling calls in the same step with `Not run: the turn ended when the user closed the question.` (deliberately NOT `CANCELED_TOOL_TEXT` — the user did not interrupt), and `break turnLoop`s into the existing `turn-complete` emit with the new `stopReason: 'question_dismissed'` — the orderly-stop path `max_steps` already established, so usage is still reported and queued messages still drain. The renderer gains one `stopReasonCopy` entry, "Question closed — waiting for you.", which is load-bearing: without it a dismissed turn is visually identical to a session that silently died. Emit surface untouched (a new stopReason *value*, not a new event type). Button label stays "Dismiss" (Destin's call). Desktop/native only — CC asks route through `hook-relay` into the CLI, which owns its own post-deny behavior, and `remote-shim.ts` hardcodes `native.supported: false`.
  **The design changed mid-flight, and the reason is the durable lesson: `deny` was carrying two incompatible meanings.** The first implementation ended the turn on any `deny` — and `harness-review-runner.test.ts:775` went red. The harness evaluator denies `AskUserQuestion` on its wrap-up turn *precisely so the model stops asking and writes the review*; ending the turn there discarded `run.review` and the eval returned empty. The spec had called this a harmless score shift; it was neither harmless nor a score shift. Specialist children (`child-ask-policy.ts`) deny for the same class of reason — nobody is there to answer. Fix locates the distinction where it is actually known: `PermissionBroker.respond()` is the only path a human's answer travels, so it stamps `dismissed: true`, and the driver ends the turn only on `behavior === 'deny' && d.dismissed`. Programmatic denies keep their prior meaning bit-for-bit — **zero changes to eval or specialist code, and the failing test passed again unmodified.** `child-ask-policy.ts` now carries the invariant in a comment: it MUST stay a bare deny.
  Guards: `harness-session-loop.test.ts` (dismissal ends the turn + history pairing; multi-call sibling back-fill; **`POLICY deny (no dismissed) → the loop CONTINUES`** — the discrimination test that makes an always-end-the-turn implementation fail), `native-permission-broker.test.ts` (stamps `dismissed` on a human deny and ONLY on a deny; a canceled ask carries none), `AssistantTurnBubble.test.tsx` (footer renders for `question_dismissed`, nothing for `end_turn`). Three pre-existing tests kept unchanged as further discrimination: ESC-cancel still emits `user-interrupt`, multi-call cancel, ordinary permission deny. Depth: `youcoded/docs/native-runtime.md` → "A dismissed question ends the turn"; spec + plan archived under `docs/archive/`.
- [x] Dead + duplicative code sweep across all five repos `#cleanup` `#renderer` `#android` `#worker` `#tests` (added 2026-07-22, **SHIPPED 2026-07-22 — youcoded `c7bb70be` (#206), `82552cee` (#207), `c9718267` (#208); wecoded-marketplace `b126c8b` (#52), `96f6566` (#53)**)
  Whole-workspace audit for dead/duplicative code. Method: `knip` on the desktop app (no dead-code tooling existed — none is checked in, this was a one-off `npx` run), `jscpd` for copy-paste, and hand tracing for Kotlin/worker/shell where no tool applies. **Every claim was re-verified by repo-wide grep before deletion** — knip's raw output had real false positives (Android Kotlin parity mirrors, comment mentions, and same-named locals all look like "usages"). Headline: the codebase is healthy — **copy-paste duplication is 0.82%** and there are **zero unreachable files** in the desktop app; the wins were concentrated, not systemic.
  **Desktop (#206):** 6 fully-dead functions (`toolByName`, `__resetGpuCacheForTests`, `getSyncService`, `getConnectionMode`, `getConnectionState`, `CloseIcon`), the redundant `export default` on all 13 `ui/` primitives (every consumer uses the named export), the redundant `@types/diff` devDependency (`diff` v9 ships its own types), and `export` dropped from **63 symbols** used only inside their own module (+ the dead `BUTTON_*` re-export line in `ui/index.ts`). **The lesson worth keeping: `tsc` was NOT a sufficient gate.** It stayed clean at 0 errors while `pruneNativePhantomRecords` broke 4 tests — `conversations-service.test.ts` reaches it via `await import('…/service')` **namespace import**, which type-checks fine against a module whose export vanished. Only the full suite caught it; that one symbol stays exported with a comment saying why. Verified 3021 tests green.
  **Android (#207):** `publishPluginViaGh`/`publishThemeViaGh` were ~110 lines of byte-identical fork→branch→recursive-upload→PR logic — extracted to one `forkPublishViaGh`, which is the refactor **desktop already had** (`github-fork-publish.ts`), so this closed real drift rather than inventing an abstraction. The unified sensitive-file list is the SUPERSET of the two (the theme copy lacked `credentials.json`/`secrets.*`). Plus **24 dead members** removed (net −202 lines), several carrying stale docstrings ("Legacy single-session API", "mirrors TS client method by method") that actively misled readers into thinking they were live — incl. `MarketplaceApiClient.getStats`, `PtyBridge.outputFlow` (no collector), the public `SessionRegistry.currentSessionId` alias, `HistoryResult.hasMore`. New `util/JsonExt.kt` `forEachKey` replaced the manual `keys()/hasNext()/next()` dance at 6 sites; sites whose body uses `continue` were deliberately left (a lambda can't `continue`). Also removed the vestigial `viewModeRequest` flow — see the note below. android-ci (tests + `assembleDebug` + R8 `assembleReleaseTest`) was the compile gate, on the belief that this host had no SDK. (**That belief was wrong — corrected 2026-08-31**: an SDK is at `/home/destin/.android-sdk`; only `ANDROID_HOME`/`local.properties` are absent. Future Android work compiles locally first.)
  **Desktop sockets (#208):** `presence-socket.ts` and `sync-hub-socket.ts` each hand-rolled the same reconnect state machine (capped backoff, 30s ping, `ws !== sock` supersede guard) — sync's own header said "Mirrors presence-socket.ts". Extracted `reconnecting-ws.ts`; the two keep their divergences as hooks (`noToken: 'wait'` vs `'poll'`, per-socket message parsing, `onTeardown` for failAllPending vs drop-snapshot). Public API byte-for-byte unchanged, so all 33 state-machine tests and the 4 external importers were untouched. Total LOC is ~flat (+14) — the win is one place to fix a reconnect bug, not fewer lines.
  **Worker (#52, #53):** `validateId()` replaced the `!id || id.length > 128` check at 4 sites; `requireAdminAccount(c)` replaced the identical `isAdminAccount → forbidden` check at 9. The admin gate stays a **per-route call, not middleware**, because `admin-middleware.ts` documents that the inline check is what keeps 401-not-authed distinct from 403-not-admin. Separately, dead `getRating()` + its `SELECT * FROM ratings` query and 4 unused row interfaces (`RatingRow` died with `getRating` — its only consumer) were removed; every query in that worker declares its own inline column shape instead, and a note in `types.ts` records that convention.
  **Two investigations that ended in "don't touch it", and are the more useful outcome:** (1) the 9 unconsumed `components/ui/` primitives (Checkbox, Radio, SegmentedTabs, AnchorTip, Toast, ProgressBar, states.tsx — ~724 LOC) are **NOT abandoned** — the UI-consistency spec says verbatim *"Building all primitives in tranche 0 keeps tranche 8 a pure migration sweep"*, and each has a numbered Destin-approved change awaiting its tranche (28, 38, 39, 44, 45, 46). Pruning them would have deleted approved work and specifically blocked the misleading-error-message audit, which waits on `ErrorState` (change 33). The two "drift" smells found en route — `FirstRunView`'s hand-rolled ProgressBar, LikeButton's local mini-toast — are *already* named migration targets of changes 46 and 44. (2) `youcoded-core`'s 2 permanently-unreachable migration scripts (2.x files stranded when the 2026-04-15 rebrand reset VERSION to 1.0.0) and 8 `/update` phases that hit "unknown phase → exit 2" (post-update.sh was slimmed 2026-04-12; `update.md` wasn't) were left alone deliberately — Phase 3 of the active deprecation plan deletes that repo wholesale, so fixing them is repairing a building with a demolition permit. **Generalizable rule both cases point at: before deleting "unused" code, ask what the PLAN for it is — staged-for-planned-work and scheduled-for-deletion look identical to a grep and want opposite actions.**
- [x] Narrow/mobile UI pass — `|||` menu, ProjectView, frame borders, Session artifacts `feature` `#remote` `#android` (added 2026-07-20, **SHIPPED 2026-07-20 — youcoded merge `f17d00cb`**)
  Mobile/remote UI was broken end-to-end below 640px, and stayed invisible because dev instances could not enable remote access to test it (fixed separately in `756ee273`). Unified on **one** breakpoint — `useNarrowViewport()` at 640px — in a renderer that had four disagreeing ones: a 700px CSS collapse, a 640px game button, 560px header labels, and a 480px hard-pinned drawer, with nothing below 700px propagating into components' own Tailwind widths. See `.claude/rules/narrow-viewport.md`.
  Reachability bugs (things that could not be reached at all, not merely ugly): **Connect 4 was unreachable** — the gamepad was `hidden sm:block` and `TOGGLE_PANEL` had exactly one caller in the entire renderer, so an incoming challenge could never be answered on a phone; the **artifact drawer's toolbar** sat outside an `overflow:hidden` box (the pane collapsed to 100% but its child `<aside>` kept a hard 480px, so ~90px hung off a 390px screen); **ProjectView's Context tab** sat behind a non-wrapping ~500px segment strip; the **detail overlay's close button** was pushed off-panel by ~340px of `shrink-0` tools; and **"Remove project"** was `opacity-0 group-hover:` — no touch path at all on the bundle phones actually run (new `.touch-reveal` utility).
  Layout: header below 640px is `|||` (settings · projects · games · session artifacts) on the left, single **target-view** chat/terminal icon on the right — it shows the view you'd switch TO, pinned by `NarrowViewToggle.test.tsx` because that reads correct either way in source and is only obviously wrong in the running app. ProjectView hero collapses to a cog and the page scrolls as one document with a sticky tab strip; both are narrow-only after Destin corrected an earlier version that applied them on desktop too.
  Frame borders on the artifact/game panes were restored after two self-inflicted breaks: `.drawer-pane` is `z-index: 11` (deliberately above `chrome-glass` at `z:10`), so with the edge spacers hidden it painted straight over both borders; and a `--chat-right-offset: 0px` override cut the chat hole to the window's right edge while the polygon's hard-coded left edge kept the left one. Fixed with margins rather than un-hiding the spacers because **`ChatView`'s shell has `.frame-edge` elements and `TerminalRightSlot`'s clone has none** — spacers would have fixed chat view and left terminal view broken.
  Naming: the session drawer is **"Session artifacts"**, superseding the older convention that reserved the word "Artifacts" for Project View; the "Session" qualifier now carries that distinction.
  **Visually verified by Destin 2026-07-20, after merge:** frame borders across the pane/view combinations, and remote projects + games sign-in on a phone. (Merged ahead of that check at his call; the check subsequently passed.)
- [x] `sync-spaces-engine` 5s `vi.waitFor` ceilings time out on macOS under load `bug` `#tests` `#ci` `#sync` (added 2026-07-19, shipped 2026-07-19)
  `desktop/tests/sync-spaces-engine.test.ts:27` ("debounces file changes into one sync (pull then push)") failed `Test timed out in 5000ms` on the `macos-latest` leg of beta run 29701441150 (2026-07-19), taking the whole beta red — `npm test` gates Build, so no `.dmg` was produced. Same family as the `subagent-watcher` / git-heavy-sync entries, but **it survives the PR #163 sweep**: that fix replaced fixed `sleep`s with `vi.waitFor` bounded-retry polling, which is right, yet `vi.waitFor` carries its OWN fixed ceiling — here `{ timeout: 5000 }`, hardcoded at six call sites in this file (lines 35, 75, 83, 105, 111, …), waiting on a real `fs.watch`-driven debounce (`debounceMs: 150`). On a loaded macOS runner FSEvents coalescing can exceed 5s. So "converted to waitFor" is NOT the same as "no fixed budget" — the sweep moved the budget, it did not remove it. **Confirmed not caused by the same-day Windows harness work** (#178/#179): this file imports only vitest/fs/os/path + `sync-spaces/engine`, with no path to `bash.ts` or `dev-tools.ts`. Not yet established whether it is a hard macOS failure or a load-dependent flake — one observation; a re-run of the failed job is the cheap discriminator. Fix shape: raise these integration ceilings the way #163 raised the git ones (30s→120s), or make the debounce assertions event-driven rather than deadline-driven. **SHIPPED 2026-07-19, youcoded PR #180.** Six inline literals collapsed to one named `WAIT_MS = 60_000`. **Verified before raising, not after** — a raised ceiling would otherwise bury a real macOS sync bug: with the new budget the WHOLE file (10 tests) runs in **3419ms** on macOS, nothing near 60s, so the assertions are fast when the runner has headroom and only blow 5s under parallel contention (a genuine engine bug would grind to the ceiling and fail anyway). Also added `macos-latest` to `desktop-ci.yml` — macOS had the identical coverage gap Windows did (only `desktop-test-build.yml` ran it), so a macOS-only failure could not surface until a beta dispatch. Both gaps are now closed.
- [x] Scoped Bash cwd persistence was broken on Windows + desktop CI never ran there `bug` `#harness` `#ci` `#native-runtime` (added 2026-07-19, shipped 2026-07-19)
  Shipped 2026-07-19, youcoded PR #178. PR #174's scoped-cwd persistence never worked on Windows: `fs.realpathSync` does **not** expand 8.3 short names, so `isInside()` compared a SHORT root (`C:\Users\RUNNER~1\…`) against the LONG form `pwd -W` reports for the same dir (`C:\Users\runneradmin\…`), `startsWith` said "outside the workspace", and the scope guard reverted **every** `cd` while emitting a spurious `Shell cwd was reset` notice on each call. Fix = `fs.realpathSync.native` (GetFinalPathNameByHandle) on both sides. **Two lessons worth more than the fix.** (1) *Vacuous tests*: two of the four cwd tests assert the cwd equals the ROOT — which is also exactly what totally-broken persistence returns, so they passed while the feature was dead. Added an explicit "no reset notice on an in-workspace `cd`" assertion that fails on the real bug. When a test's expected value coincides with the broken value, it is not a test. (2) *`desktop-ci.yml` was `runs-on: ubuntu-latest` only*, so these tests had never executed on Windows and #174 merged green; the failure only surfaced at BETA-BUILD time, because `desktop-test-build.yml` was the sole workflow with a Windows runner. CI now has a `windows-latest` matrix leg (tests only; packaging stays Linux). Diagnosis required a temporary diagnostic pushed to CI to dump real runner state — reasoning about Windows path semantics from Linux produced a wrong first guess.
- [x] Git Bash detection on Windows: too narrow, too early, and silent `bug` `#harness` `#native-runtime` `#install` (added 2026-07-19, shipped 2026-07-19)
  Shipped 2026-07-19, youcoded PR #179. `detectShell()` (`tools/bash.ts`) probed exactly two hardcoded `Program Files` paths, resolved at module import, and logged nothing on fallback — and it gates `tracksCwd`, so a PowerShell fallback silently degrades **every** local/OpenRouter session (`CORE_TOOLS` is attached unconditionally at `native-session-host.ts:216`; native is on by default and reachable from the remote server too). Three fixes: (1) *narrow* → derive `bash.exe` from the resolved `git.exe`, honor Claude Code's documented `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`, consult `which` — while explicitly REFUSING `System32\bash.exe`, the WSL launcher, which would run commands in a Linux VM against a Windows cwd (worse than PowerShell); (2) *early* → `main.ts:8 → ipc-handlers.ts:31 → bash.ts` all import eagerly, so a user whose git was installed BY first-run (`winget install Git.Git`) stayed pinned to PowerShell for that entire session; detection is now lazy + memoized, resolving on first use; (3) *silent* → warns with the remedy, and `dev-tools.ts` reports a `harness shell` probe (`ok:false` on PowerShell) so it lands in bug reports. First test coverage `detectShell()` has ever had (8 cases, win32 branch driven from any platform via mocked `fs`/`which`); needed `path.win32` for the git.exe derivation and a static `which` import to be testable at all. **Deliberately NOT a hard prerequisite:** Claude Code itself treats Git for Windows as optional with a real PowerShell *tool* as fallback ([setup docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup.md)); this makes detection reliable enough that the PowerShell path should be near-empty, and now measurable. Revisit a hard gate only if diagnostics say it is still happening.

Pixels unchanged; the one real behaviour change is that all 22 now carry `role="alert"`, so a screen reader announces errors that were previously silent

- [x] Reading a long conversation back to its beginning no longer costs gigabytes `performance` `#chat`
  (added 2026-08-28, **SHIPPED 2026-09-03 — youcoded PR #398, merge `b246a57a`**). Perf cycle 3.
  Cycle 2 made a conversation OPEN at its last 30 turns but never bounded how large one could
  BECOME: a page loaded by scrolling up was prepended and nothing removed it. A message more than
  ~1.5 screens outside the viewport now renders as a spacer of the height its body last occupied;
  nothing leaves the reducer, so scroll height is unchanged, nothing is re-fetched, and unfolding
  is a render. Measured 3 repeats each side, same rig, baseline at the branch's parent commit:
  ceiling PSS **4,346 → 1,784 MB (−59%)**, DOM nodes added **+1,441,274 → −6,348**, JS heap added
  513 → 64 MB, session switch p95 243 → 112 ms, main-thread blocking 4,882 → 1,703 ms, page turn
  medium/small 885/1,000 → 384/158 ms. No regressions (startup ≤1.8%, idle PSS +0.1%, history
  ≤0.3%, stall identical, artifacts ≤2.4%). **Eviction was specced and REJECTED on independent
  review** — six blocking defects, two structural: deleted uuids stay in `seenUuids` so scrolling
  back replays nothing, and ~15 readers scan the session-lifetime tool maps. `content-visibility:
  auto` was separately closed (its implicit `contain: paint` clips theme glows). **Four bugs in the
  change were caught by measurement or by Destin, none by review** — the last, messages popping in
  while scrolling, survived three clean measurement runs because the rig measures memory and counts,
  never smoothness. Spec + the rejected design:
  `docs/archive/specs/2026-08-28-cycle-3-bounding-the-conversation-window.md`. What remains:
  `docs/active/handoffs/2026-09-03-perf-next-steps-handoff.md`
- [x] 2026-09-03 dev-workspace — The Android build labels the vendored terminal emulator GPLv3 while its own VENDORED.md says Apache 2.0 (VENDORED.md was right: Termux's LICENSE.md at v0.118.1 carves terminal-emulator out as Apache 2.0, and Destin is the sole author of app/, so the whole repo is now MIT — youcoded#400; the wider formalization plan is docs/active/investigations/2026-09-03-formalization-costs-and-risks.md)
- [x] 2026-09-03 dev-workspace — File-watching tests went red on the macOS CI leg every week or two, always with zero watcher events, on branches touching nothing related (youcoded#399) — cause is that macOS `fs.watch()` RETURNS BEFORE THE OS WATCH IS ARMED, so a change in that window is never reported at all; Linux and Windows arm inside the call. Measured with a dependency-free probe on a 3-core runner: 4 missed of 200 immediate writes under CPU load, 0 of 200 at a 25ms gap, 0 of 200 on Linux at any gap. Both watchers now reconcile once on subscribe, since there is no "armed" signal to wait for. Real app defect too, not just CI: a commit made just after a project opened went unnoticed. 4/15 red before, 0/15 after, same runner and load. Three more watcher sites share the cause and are filed unfixed

- [x] 2026-09-04 claude-code-integration — Every new Claude Code session opened with a "No such file or directory" hook error from the deleted youcoded-core clone (youcoded `a467c3e8`; legacy-cleanup.ts deletes that folder at launch and its docstring claimed the following reconcileHooks() pass stripped the orphaned settings.json entries — it never could, because pruneDeadPluginHooks only recognises a hook as plugin-owned if its script resolves under a root returned by listInstalledPluginDirs(), which only walks directories that still EXIST. Deleting the clone is exactly what made its entries unprunable, so the error was permanent for every upgrading user. Fixed by treating the legacy path as an app-owned prune root whether or not it exists; the file-exists check and the "never remove user-added hooks" guarantee are unchanged. Android already pruned this path in Bootstrap.kt, so desktop was the only gap. Destin's own settings had been cleaned by the 2026-09-03 brain restructure, so the symptom was already gone on his machine before the fix) → docs/archive/investigations/2026-09-01-stale-session-start-hook.md
- [x] 2026-09-04 games — Connect 4 could not be played without a mouse: its columns were plain divs with mouse handlers only, so nothing took a tab stop, Enter and Space did nothing, and assistive tech had no name for a column (youcoded `424d5f9b`). Columns are now real buttons with the tab-stop gating ChessBoard already used per square (only a column you can actually play takes a stop, and it is never `disabled` since a disabled button cannot be focused or read); each is labelled with its occupancy ("Column 3, 2 of 6 filled, drop here" / "Column 4, full"); focus shows the ghost disc the pointer shows; and an sr-only role=status region narrates the last drop and whose turn it is, matching the primitive 2048 and Flappy already use. `data-col` preserved for the UI review rig. 11 pinning cases in tests/connect-four-keyboard.test.tsx, 9 of which fail against the pre-fix board → docs/archive/investigations/2026-09-01-connect4-keyboard-play.md
